- Spend (#317): the 80% warning and cap-stop details now reach only the owner, by DM, and only a running bridge sends them; a setup running only `github watch` or the daemon keeps the warning pending in `spend_alerts` until a bridge runs (sent only if that is within 24 h and spend is still at 80% or more), a WATCH run's cap stop is never DMed (its GitHub comment says only `Work is paused for budget.`), and a cap stop's details that could not be DMed are held in memory, so a bridge restart loses them (the channel ping, the owner's `/status` and doctor still show the cap). Non-owners no longer see amounts in posts or `/status`, and WATCH spend-cap comments now read `Work is paused for budget.`
- Schedules (#322): from now on a schedule whose run asks anything waits at each slot until the owner or its creator presses **Cancel** or answers — a spend-cap stop included, which offers **Cancel** only; watch for the one `⏸️ … waiting …` note. At the update only a question no bridge had posted yet carries over and blocks (it is posted with its buttons); questions already posted are closed as `superseded` and hold nothing. A schedule auto-paused (or paused by a SAFE-13 refusal, #307) from now on needs `/schedule resume` and an answer or **Cancel** on its pause question; resume alone leaves it waiting (its reply still shows the next slot). A run that cannot start — for example #311's schedule on a nested checkout whose origin is off the GitHub allowlist — now holds its schedule at the first failure with the stuck question instead of being auto-paused after 5 failed runs: allowlist the repo and then answer or **Cancel** the question so the next slot runs, or `/schedule delete` it. A schedule with no channel now sends its questions and note to the owner by DM; with no owner configured it waits until an owner is set (restart the bridge; the question is then DMed) or `/schedule delete`. Only the creator and the owner can answer (not other admins or team members), `/schedule list` shows no waiting marker, a closed question's post keeps its buttons (a later press gets `This choice isn’t for you (or it was already answered).`), a channel-less schedule's DM that fails is retried every tick with no log line, and a box running only `corvidinho daemon` (no bridge) skips such a schedule's slots until a bridge posts the question and someone answers or cancels it, and nothing runs at the press: the next due slot does.
- GitHub (#313): a stuck WATCH run reaches the owner by DM only when a bridge runs on the same `CORVIDINHO_DATA_DIR` as `github watch` (within a day), the owner has a Discord id (`[owner]` `discord_id` or `CORVIDINHO_OWNER_DISCORD_ID`, as seen by both `github watch` and the bridge) and accepts DMs from server members; the owner answers on the GitHub thread. A community run that keeps hitting the same refusal now ends in a stuck question that pings the owner, on Discord and on GitHub alike.
- Other behaviour operators will see: a run that repeats a failing call gets the `[Corvidinho harness — AGENT-16] …` steer and, on the next identical call, stops `blocked` with `The same <tool> call keeps failing with nothing changed in between. How should I proceed?` (#313); `/status` shows `LLM: none — …` when no model is set (setting names for the owner only) and spend amounts only to the owner, anyone else at most `Spend: Work is paused for budget.` (#320, #317); the Discord thinking status can read `⏳ waiting for the owner's OK on an Approve card` for up to 5 minutes (#319); verified runs report `Verify gate: <n> test(s) ran (bun test: <n>), and none were deleted.` and `/work` can refuse its PR with `tests-deleted` (#321); a DM over 1900 characters, or a component reply, form reply or message edit over 2000, is now refused and logged instead of cut, and the private Choose list is cut at 1900 with `…` (#316).
- Rollback to 0.0.35: it opens the v15 DB without complaint and ignores `approval_requests`, `approval_codes`, `forget_requests.action_hash`, the `schedule_runs` ask columns, `watch_owner_asks` and `discord_bridge_runner`. It reads `CORVIDINHO_LLM_MODEL` as one plain OpenAI-compatible model name (a `kind:` prefix or comma list included), ignores `OLLAMA_HOST` and `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, and falls back to `gpt-4o-mini` when no model is set and to the demo stub when no key is. It drops the must-ask gate (posts, default-branch pushes and prod commands run with no card; open `mustask` / `mustask-post` cards are not handled), approves a forget request on one press with no code and no check that the counts still match — decide open forget asks before rolling back — puts spend amounts and the 80% warning back in public posts and `/status`, and restores the old WATCH spend-cap comment. Schedules waiting on a question run again at their next slot, their questions can no longer be answered or cancelled, and a channel-less schedule's questions are not delivered. Verify passes again without test evidence, the repeat-failure guard is gone, stuck WATCH asks are no longer DMed, and the gateway cuts over-long DMs and edits again. With `CORVIDINHO_BACKUP_DIR` set, 0.0.35's nightly backup refuses the v15 DB (`snapshot check failed: schema version 15 is newer than this build (13)`, the owner pinged once per failure streak in `/announce`), and its `backup restore` refuses a 0.0.36 snapshot (`snapshot <name> is not usable: schema version 15 is newer than this build (13)`).

## 0.0.35

### Knows everyone (M1)

- **On GitHub, people and the owner are matched only by their numeric user id; `[owner]` gains `github_id`, and `/admin people link github:` looks the id up and stores it** — [#303](https://github.com/CorvidLabs/Corvidinho/pull/303) (IDENTITY-7.a, IDENTITY-7; REQ-discord-367, REQ-watch-367, REQ-cli-367, REQ-discord-036, REQ-discord-042, REQ-discord-067, REQ-watch-036, REQ-watch-071, REQ-watch-067, REQ-plugins-067, REQ-agent-071): a GitHub login matched a declared person (the match was dropped only when GitHub reported a numeric id and the person had declared other `github_ids`), and `[owner] github_login` made a commenter the owner, so a renamed or re-registered login could pass as someone. `resolvePerson` now matches GitHub on the numeric user id alone (`github_ids`; a login passed to it is ignored): a commenter with no id, or an id nobody declared, is undeclared (community) and never the owner, whatever their login. Every GitHub recognition path uses the event's `senderId` only: the WATCH `[Corvidinho acting GitHub user …]` block (its header now says `recognised from the owner's people list by GitHub numeric user id only, never by a login or a name`, and an undeclared commenter's line reads `- declared_person: none (this GitHub user id is not on the owner's people list; a login or a name never makes someone a declared person)`), the WATCH memory inject, the in-run memory plugins (`CORVIDINHO_ACTING_GITHUB_ID`) and the SAFE-13 owner exemption for WATCH titles and bodies. With only the owner configured, a commenter who uses a login someone holds as a label (the owner's) without their id now gets that `declared_person: none` block instead of the owner's `role: owner` block (any other undeclared login still gets no block). Logins stay as labels: shown in the block, used to @mention the owner on GitHub, used by forget-me cleanup, and a login already linked to someone else is still refused on link. New `[owner] github_id` is read from the allowlist file only (TOML quoted or bare, JSON string or safe integer; digits only, and `0` is not an id, so the example's `github_id = "0"` placeholder is never read as one); an invalid value is ignored with an issue that does not echo it; the id is added to the owner's person, declared or built-in, and `isOwnerGithub` compares that id only. `/admin people link person:<id> github:<login>` plans the request first (a conflict or an undeclared person is refused with its own reason and no call), then defers its ephemeral reply and looks the login up once (`GET /users/{login}` through the GitHub API, with `GITHUB_TOKEN` / `GH_TOKEN` when set, 10 s timeout) and stores the numeric id in `github_ids` next to the login; a failed lookup, no such user, or an answer for a different login links nothing, writes one `error` audit row and replies `Refused: could not look up the GitHub numeric user id of @<login> (…). On GitHub people match only by that id (IDENTITY-7.a). Nothing changed — try again, or link github_id:<number>.` Linking a login that is already linked adds its id, which is how existing entries are migrated. `unlink github:<login>` looks nothing up, removes only the label and, while an id stays linked, adds `GitHub id … stays linked, so GitHub still recognises them — unlink github_id to stop that (IDENTITY-7.a).` `corvidinho doctor` prints `[warn] people-github` naming, by person id only (`the owner` for the built-in entry), everyone with a GitHub login but no numeric id; it never fails doctor. Entries with only `github_logins` keep loading and still match on Discord; they are not recognised on GitHub until an id is linked. Unchanged and still login-based: the WATCH user allowlist (`[github] users`, ALLOW-1), the assignment / review-request actor gate, and the owner @mention on SAFE-13 and summary comments (`[owner] github_login`). No schema change.
- **Community members can't start `/work`** — [#300](https://github.com/CorvidLabs/Corvidinho/pull/300) (IDENTITY-11.a, IDENTITY-11; REQ-discord-065, REQ-discord-088): since #285 community could run a read-only `/work`: a declared community member or an undeclared user got a public deferred reply, a session, a git worktree, a `talk/*` branch, a work task and an agent run with the verify lane, with only the edit tools and the PR step withheld. `/work` now resolves the caller's role with `resolveDiscordActingRole` (the people list re-read on every command, the owner as the bridge loaded it at start, the caller's Discord role ids included) and refuses community — declared `role = "community"`, a declared person with no role, and anyone undeclared — with the ephemeral `not authorized` that `/announce channel`, `/schedule create` and `/admin` send a non-owner, before `deferReply` and before any worktree, branch, work task, run, verify or PR step; no audit row is written. The empty-description check (`Please provide a task description.`) and the SAFE-13 check still come first, so an injected community `/work` still gets the SAFE-13 refusal and pings the owner. A muted or deny-listed caller is refused earlier by the dispatcher (the mute reply or the zero-width ack). Owner and team `/work` is unchanged, and a role change in the people file applies to the next `/work` without a restart. With no owner configured only a declared team member can start `/work`; with nobody declared team, nobody can. `/work` stays registered and visible to every guild member. The PR step's "changes stay on the work branch" line can now reach only a team member demoted during the run. `/session start` and chat stay open to community with read tools only.
- **Private notes, profiles and the owner's view of someone's memory go only to the person who asked, by DM, and the model never sees their text** — [#302](https://github.com/CorvidLabs/Corvidinho/pull/302) (MEMORY-7.a, MEMORY-7; REQ-plugins-710, REQ-agent-710, REQ-cli-710, REQ-discord-710): a private read came back to the model as the tool result, and only a prompt rule kept it out of the channel. In a Discord conversation (a run with a reply channel: chat, button-pick and Answer-form resumes, `/session start`, `/work`), `memory-recall --category private`, the owner's `memory-recall --person` view and `memory-profile` (own or `--person`) now return their text only as `privateText`: the model, the tool message and the run's events get `sent privately (MEMORY-7.a): this result went only to the person who asked, in a direct message, and is not shown to you — do not guess or repeat what it says; tell them it was sent to them privately`, so nothing the model writes (the answer, an ask, a tool argument, a file, a PR, a stored memory, a later turn) can repeat it. `task run` carries the text in a new `privateReplies` list on its json / ndjson result frame: a retried attempt's repeat kept once, at most 5 entries, each secret-scrubbed and then cut to 6000 characters with `… (cut here — ask for a narrower part to see the rest)`, the last one saying how many more were not sent, so large private reads cannot push the frame past the 1 MiB line cap; the Discord agent client re-checks the same bounds. The bridge sends each one by DM to whoever asked (the forget card's `sendDm` path) under `🔒 Private — only you can see this (MEMORY-7.a):`, scrubbed, `@everyone` / `@here` defanged and split into parts of at most 1900; private listings show up to 500 characters per row. The channel answer starts with `🔒 The private part was sent to you in a DM — it is never shown in a shared channel.`, or, when any part of the DM did not go out, `🔒 The private part is never shown in a shared channel, and I couldn't DM it to you — open your DMs to me and ask again.`, and the text is shown nowhere else (DM parts that already went out stay in the DM); the session thread never records it. Where there is no private place these reads are refused: in a schedule run (`refused: a profile or the owner's view of someone's memory is shown only privately to that person or the owner, in a conversation with them — never in a schedule or other run's channel (MEMORY-7.a)`; private notes keep their MEMORY-7 refusal) and in a GitHub thread, where `memory-profile` is now refused too (`refused: a profile is shown only privately to that person or the owner, never in a GitHub thread — it is public; ask on Discord (MEMORY-7.a)`). The local CLI without a role session is unchanged; `plugins run memory-profile` in a role session (`CORVIDINHO_ACTING_IS_ADMIN` set) with no `CORVIDINHO_DISCORD_REPLY_CHANNEL_ID` is refused like a schedule. A person's everyday recall of their own non-private rows and the automatic memory inject still reach the model (MEMORY-8, MEMORY-9), and the owner's `memory-forget` / `memory-override` confirmation is unchanged. No config key, env var, table or schema change.
- **Someone known only on GitHub can ask there to be forgotten, and the owner can start a forget for any declared person with `/admin people forget`; either way only Approve on the owner's card deletes anything** — [#301](https://github.com/CorvidLabs/Corvidinho/pull/301) (MEMORY-ACL-6.a, MEMORY-ACL-6; REQ-discord-1016, REQ-watch-1016, REQ-plugins-1016): forget-me worked only from Discord (`memory-forget-me` refused in WATCH runs), and the owner could not start one. WATCH now handles a clear "forget me" itself, with no model run: an issue body, issue comment or PR review comment that @mentions the watch user and says only a short ask such as `forget me`, `forget about me` or `forget / delete / erase everything (you know) about me` (a greeting, `please`, `can / could you` and `thanks` allowed; quoted lines ignored; anything more goes to a normal run). The repo and user allowlist gates still apply, and the ask is taken out before the per-issue dedupe, so it never hides another request on the same issue. The sender is matched in the people list by GitHub numeric id only. A declared person gets the same `forget_requests` ask a Discord ask creates (SAFE-5 `memory-forget-request` `started` then `ok` as `github:<login>`, fail closed; one open ask per person; nothing deleted), and the bridge DMs the owner the existing Approve/Deny card, which names `asked on GitHub by @<login> (GitHub account id <n>) in <owner/repo>#<n>` and, once decided, says `They will be told on their GitHub thread.`; the bridge never DMs a GitHub asker. Each such comment gets one reply on the thread: the request went to the owner (`… no answer within 24 h means no. I'll post the outcome here. (MEMORY-ACL-6.a)`) or is already waiting; `You're not on the owner's people list, so I keep no memory or profile for this GitHub account …` for an undeclared sender (no card; their words in kept WATCH conversations expire after 30 days); `I can't confirm this GitHub account is the person on the owner's people list (people are matched by GitHub account id, never by login alone) …` for a login on the list without a matching id (no card); `No owner is configured …`; or `I couldn't record your forget request right now …`. Once the owner decides, the next poll posts the outcome on that thread (approved, not approved, or no answer in time; never a count or any content) while the repo stays allowlisted; a rate limit or a post with no HTTP answer stops that cycle's outcome posts, while a locked or deleted thread does not hold up anyone else's and is given up a day after the decision. Log lines: `[watch] forget-me <repo>#<n> id=<id> @<login>: <outcome>`, `[watch] forget-me reply failed …`, `[watch] forget outcome <status> posted …`, `[watch] forget outcome post failed …`, `[watch] forget request <id>: thread not reachable; the outcome was not posted`, `[watch] forget outcomes read failed: …`. `memory-forget-me` in a WATCH run still refuses, now telling the person to post such a comment. New owner-only `/admin people forget person:<id>` (the dispatch floor plus the handler's own re-check; declared person ids only, never a Discord id or the built-in `owner` entry) is audited like the other people commands (SAFE-5 `admin-people-forget` `started` then `ok`, `denied` for an id that is not a declared person, refused with nothing asked when there is no audit trail or no database), records the same ask (an open one is reused: `No change: … already has an open forget request …`) and sends the card at once, marked `started by you with /admin people forget`; nobody else is told, and it replies `✅ /admin people forget: asked to forget <who> (request <id>). Approve or Deny it on the card I DM you — nothing is forgotten until you approve, and no answer within 24 h means no.` Approve now also reaches the person's declared GitHub numeric ids (their declared logins it already reached) plus the login and id a GitHub ask came from (a WATCH run's kept conversation now records `github-id:<n>` next to the login), so their memory, session turns and kept conversations, WATCH threads included, are deleted; an `/admin` forget never adds the owner who started it as a target (only naming the owner's own declared entry would reach their memory). Who asked is stored in the existing `requester_user_id` column (a Discord id, `github:<id>:<login>` or `admin:<owner id>`) and a GitHub thread in `origin_channel_id` (`github:<owner/repo>#<n>`); no schema change, config key or env var.

### Talk anywhere (M2)

- **The bridge's restart post is one short line in the persona's voice linking that version's release notes, not a CHANGELOG dump** — [#305](https://github.com/CorvidLabs/Corvidinho/pull/305) (PERSONA-1.a, PERSONA-1, DISCORD-ANNOUNCE-4; REQ-discord-024, REQ-discord-025): on every ClientReady the bridge posted `bridge live **vX.Y.Z**` followed by up to five CHANGELOG bullets to the `/announce` channel (838 characters for 0.0.34). It now posts one fixed line, `Back online and running **v0.0.35** 🐦‍⬛ Everything new in this version is in the release notes 👀 <https://github.com/CorvidLabs/Corvidinho/releases/tag/v0.0.35>`: under 200 characters, no bullets, headings or newlines, nothing read from CHANGELOG.md, no model call and no spend, SAFE-6 scrubbed with `@everyone` / `@here` defanged, and the link wrapped in `<>` so Discord shows no preview card. Only a plain `X.Y.Z` version is echoed; anything else gets `Back online 🐦‍⬛ Everything new is in the release notes 👀 <https://github.com/CorvidLabs/Corvidinho/releases>` and the input is never shown. It is still one post, to the announcements channel only (none set, nothing posted), after every restart, including one on the same version. The drafted `persona.md` voice is confirmed as it is (no file change); the line is a hand-written template, so editing `persona.md` does not change it. `scripts/corvidinho-update.sh` still posts nothing to Discord, and the GitHub Release notes the link opens still come from this CHANGELOG. No config key, env var, slash command or schema change.

### Real dev teammate (M3)

- **Verification can't be skipped, the real git diff decides what changed, and a talk whose last run did not end verified re-verifies every edit since the talk started** — [#308](https://github.com/CorvidLabs/Corvidinho/pull/308) (AGENT-14, AGENT-15, AGENT-15.a; REQ-agent-015, REQ-agent-002, REQ-agent-003, REQ-agent-085, REQ-agent-242, REQ-agent-502, REQ-cli-006, REQ-cli-007, REQ-cli-009, REQ-cli-073, REQ-cli-085, REQ-cli-143, REQ-cli-505, REQ-discord-001, REQ-discord-014, REQ-discord-073, REQ-discord-085, REQ-watch-006, REQ-watch-073, REQ-watch-085): the CLI took `--no-verify`, a project's `fledge.toml` could set `[corvidinho] verify_before_complete = false`, `filesChanged` kept every path a tool reported even when git showed no change (the real diff was only added on top, and with the key off no git snapshot was taken), and edits left by a run that ended blocked, failed or cancelled (or whose process died) were never verified by the next run in that talk. `--no-verify` is removed: wherever Corvidinho would read it as its own flag, on any command (`--no-verify doctor` too), it stops before anything runs and exits 1 with `corvidinho: --no-verify was removed: verification can't be skipped (AGENT-14)` and `hint: run the command without it; the verify gate runs only when the run changed something` on stderr (with `--json`, `{ ok: false, error }` on stdout); `--task --no-verify` is still task text and `plugins run <name> -- --no-verify` still reaches the plugin; help and the `task run` usage line drop it. `[corvidinho] verify_before_complete` is ignored (Corvidinho's own `fledge.toml` drops it), and `corvidinho doctor` prints `[warn] verify-gate: fledge.toml [corvidinho] verify_before_complete is ignored — verification can't be turned off (AGENT-14); remove the key` for a project that still sets it, never failing doctor. `runTask` always snapshots the git work tree: in a git project `filesChanged` holds only paths in the real diff (still the union across attempts, capped at 1000); a path a tool says it changed but git does not show (a gitignored file, a nested repo, a write that changed nothing) is left out of `filesChanged` but still runs the verify lane, with one `Verify gate: <n> path(s) a tool reported changing are not in the git diff (…), so they are not listed as changed, but verifying anyway.` note; once a verify has failed, every later attempt of that run is verified again; a run that changed nothing ends `done` with `verifySkipped` and exactly one `Verify gate: no changes, nothing to verify.` note. The demo stub (no LLM key) no longer claims `src/cli.ts`, so a keyless `task run` in the caller's own checkout ends `done` with the no-changes note instead of running the verify lane. A non-git project, or a start snapshot git cannot read, keeps tool-reported files and the REQ-agent-502 fail-closed rule. A talk worktree (git admin dir `worktrees/talk-*`, as Discord and schedule talks are named) keeps a `corvidinho-verified` marker file in its own git dir, never part of the diff: `ensureTalkWorkspace` writes it when it creates the worktree, and each run removes it at start and writes it back only when it ends `done`. When the marker is missing, the run's baseline is the talk branch's merge-base with the remote's default branch or `main` (the `/work` `resolveBase` rule, moved to `src/worktree/base.ts`), so every edit since the talk started, an earlier attempt's included, is verified before `done`, with one `Verify gate: the last run in this talk did not end verified, so every edit since the talk started is checked (from the talk branch's merge-base).` note; if git cannot find the base branch the run verifies anyway. A new talk, and a run after a `done` run, start from their own snapshot; a run in the caller's own checkout keeps the run-start baseline. `delegate` / `council` workers (`CORVIDINHO_DELEGATE_DEPTH` above 0) run nested in their lead's talk worktree: they never take or write the marker, keep their own run-start baseline, and remove a marker when they do not end `done`; the lead's gate covers the combined change. Chat, button resumes, `/session`, `/work`, schedules, WATCH and delegate workers all start `task run`, so they share this one gate. Not in this release: the second half of AGENT-15 ("tests ran and none were deleted"), so a lane with no tests, or with deleted tests, can still pass as verified.

### Safe autonomy (M4)

- **A non-owner's answer typed into the private Answer form is scanned and fenced like their chat reply** — [#299](https://github.com/CorvidLabs/Corvidinho/pull/299) (SAFE-12, SAFE-13, DISCORD-ASK-4.a; REQ-discord-548, REQ-discord-071): #294's form (merged after #295) put the typed text straight into the resumed run's prompt, so a community user's injection payload typed there started a normal run with no refusal, owner ping or audit row, while the same words sent as a chat reply were refused. After the thin-answer and cancel checks (the same order as chat), a team or community requester's submit now goes through the SAFE-13 detector. A hit starts no run, leaves the ask pending and the session live, and records no turn: the submit gets the ephemeral `🛡️ I won't act on that: it looks like a prompt-injection attempt (it …). I've flagged it to the owner. (SAFE-13)`, which never quotes the text; one fresh post in the session's channel, replying to the ask's stub with allowed mentions set to the owner only, reads `🛡️ <@owner> heads-up: an answer typed in the private Answer form here looked like a prompt-injection attempt (it …); I didn't act on it. (SAFE-13)` and is tracked on the session as chat's refusal is; and one `injection-suspected` / `denied` row is written (surface `discord:<session>`; `ask-answer` and the reason ids are only in its args digest). An accepted answer reaches the model inside the `UNTRUSTED_DATA` fence (`role: team` / `role: community`, `source=ask-answer`), while `humanText`, the memory query and the recorded turn keep the scrubbed answer. The owner's answer is neither fenced nor scanned. The button and form path now also resolves the presser's role with their Discord role ids, as chat, `/session start` and `/work` do, so a declared team member allowlisted only through a Discord role (with a non-empty user or role allowlist) now resumes as team there instead of community; nobody gets more than their chat message already gets. No env var, config key, table or schema change.
- **A non-owner's picked Choose label reaches the run inside the untrusted fence, and a pressed option id the ask doesn't have answers `that choice expired`** — [#310](https://github.com/CorvidLabs/Corvidinho/pull/310) (SAFE-12.a, SAFE-12; REQ-discord-548, REQ-discord-071): a pick sent the picked option's label to `Human answer:` unfenced for everyone, although the model may have copied that label from a non-owner's own fenced words; and when no option matched the pressed id, the bridge fell back to the raw option id from the button. A team or community presser's label now goes through `fenceSpeakerText(label, role, "ask-pick")`, with the role resolved at press time (Discord role ids included, as #299 above); the label is fenced but not scanned by the SAFE-13 detector; `humanText`, the memory query and the recorded thread turn keep the plain label, and the owner's resumed prompt is byte-identical to before. The raw-id fallback is gone: an option id that matches none of the ask's options (a forged or stale id, or a pick id on a free-text ask) gets only the ephemeral `that choice expired`, before the ask is claimed and for everyone, the owner included; nothing runs, is posted or is recorded, and the ask stays open for a real pick, the Answer form or a reply. Resume, expiry, the buttons cleared at once with `Got it — **<label>**. Working on it…`, and that ack deleted when the run ends are unchanged. No env var, config key, table or schema change.
- **A schedule's text is its creator's words: a non-owner's `/schedule create` is scanned, and every tick re-resolves the creator, fences a non-owner's prompt and refuses a stored injection** — [#307](https://github.com/CorvidLabs/Corvidinho/pull/307) (SAFE-12, SAFE-13; REQ-discord-713): the scheduler passed `schedule.prompt` to the run exactly as stored, with no fence and no scan at create or at run time, so a schedule stored while admin lists still granted ADMIN, or made by someone who is no longer the owner, was replayed on every tick unchecked (its runs were already non-ADMIN and stamped community). `/schedule create` now resolves the requester's role as `/work` does and, before the ADMIN gate, scans a non-owner's `name` and `prompt`: a hit stores nothing and goes through `refuseInjectedSlash` — an ephemeral refusal that never quotes the text, one fresh channel post pinging only the owner (`🛡️ <@owner> heads-up: a /schedule request here looked like a prompt-injection attempt (it …); I didn't act on it. (SAFE-13)`) and one `injection-suspected` / `denied` row (surface `discord:/schedule`; `schedule-prompt` is only in its args digest). A non-owner create that trips nothing still gets the quiet ephemeral `not authorized`, and the owner's own words are never scanned. On every tick, after the DISCORD-SCHEDULE-3 gate, the creator's role is resolved again with `resolveDiscordActingRole` from the live allowlist, the owner, the bridge's mute set and the people list read at that moment (a tick has no Discord role ids, so a creator allowlisted only through a Discord role is still stopped by that gate, as before). When a non-owner's stored name, description or prompt trips the detector, nothing runs and no worktree is made: the log gets `[scheduler] SAFE-13: schedule <id> not run: its text looks like a prompt-injection attempt (<reasons>); pausing it`; the bridge's ticker writes one `denied` row (surface `scheduler:<id>`; a `corvidinho daemon` tick writes none, having no audit trail wired); the run is recorded failed (`not run: the schedule's text looks like a prompt-injection attempt (…) (SAFE-13)`) with the stuck ask `🛡️ I didn't run this schedule: its text looks like a prompt-injection attempt (it …). I paused it; /schedule delete removes it. (SAFE-13)`; the schedule is paused, so later ticks neither run nor repeat it; and that ask goes out through the usual ask path, pinging the owner once (a daemon tick leaves it pending and a bridge posts it). When the name is what tripped, the ask is titled by id and project only, without the name; every other schedule post keeps its name. A schedule with no channel has no post, so its refusal is the log line, the audit row, the run's ask and the paused status. Otherwise a non-owner creator's run starts `Scheduled work on project: <project>` and gets `Schedule "<name>":` and the prompt inside the `UNTRUSTED_DATA` fence (`role: team`, or `role: community` for a muted team member or anyone else; `source=schedule-prompt`). The owner's schedules keep exactly the prompt they had. Result posts, ping keys, auto-pause and the delivery pass are unchanged. No env var, config key, table or schema change.
- **Ask questions and choice labels are secret-scrubbed before they are cut or posted** — [#306](https://github.com/CorvidLabs/Corvidinho/pull/306) (SAFE-6.a, SAFE-6; REQ-discord-066, REQ-agent-045): `normalizeQuestion` cut a question at 1500 (`ASK_QUESTION_MAX`) and each choice label was cut at 80 before any scrub, so a key split by the cut kept its prefix plus fewer raw characters than its pattern's minimum and every later scrub (the stored `pending_ask`, `schedule_runs.ask_question`, the posts) missed it; and `buildChoiceComponents` posted `label.slice(0, 80)` with no scrub at all, so a label holding a whole key showed raw on the Choose buttons. A question is now control-stripped and trimmed, SAFE-6 scrubbed, then cut at 1500; a label (`cleanAskLabel`) is whitespace-collapsed, scrubbed, then cut at 80 (`ASK_OPTION_LABEL_MAX`); a cut question or label is scrubbed once more, because the cut can end a key shape (an AWS key id), and that marker is shorter than what it replaces, so the text stays within its cap and normalizing it again changes nothing. Every ask goes through it: the tool loop, the result frame of chat, `/work`, `/session start` and schedule runs, and a stored ask reloaded after a restart; the Choose buttons clean each label again when they are posted, with their `custom_id`s byte-identical. A secret-looking option id still becomes its position (#265). A cut that falls inside the marker keeps only its start (`…[redacted:gith…`); no piece of the secret ever shows. No `SCRUB_RULES_VERSION` bump: a split piece already stored by an earlier build is too short for any rule, so a re-scrub cannot find it. No env var, config key, command or schema change.
- **`.fledge/` is SAFE-2 protected like `fledge.toml` and `specs/`, so a run can't rewrite or delete the verify lane it is judged by** — [#304](https://github.com/CorvidLabs/Corvidinho/pull/304) (SAFE-2.a, SAFE-2; REQ-plugins-083, REQ-discord-476): Fledge reads the `verify` lane from `fledge.toml` or a `.fledge/lanes/*.toml` import; `fledge.toml` was protected but `.fledge/` was not, so `files-write .fledge/lanes/verify.toml "[lanes.verify] steps=[]"` or a `files-delete` of it returned `ok: true`. `isProtectedPath` now refuses any `.fledge` path component, anywhere in the path and in any letter case: `files-write`, `files-edit` and `files-delete` (the same tools owner and team `/work` runs and `delegate` workers use) check the path as given and where it resolves, symlinks and dangling links followed, and exit 2 with the SAFE-2 text, whose list now reads `(.env* / .git / fledge.toml / .fledge / bunfig.toml / specs / *.spec.md / .specsync / keystores)`; `git-commit` refuses to stage a `.fledge/` deletion; `discord-send-file` refuses to attach anything under `.fledge/`. Reads stay allowed (`files-read`, `files-list`, `files-glob`, `search-grep`, `fledge-lanes-list` / `fledge-lanes-validate`), and `docs/fledge.md`, `fledge/…` and `.fledgerc` are not protected. A project checked out under a `.fledge/` directory becomes read-only to the file tools. Not covered by SAFE-2.a: `shell-exec` (its #309 SAFE-21 checks refuse `>` / `tee` / `sed -i` writes anywhere, but not `cp` into `.fledge/` or an in-worktree `rm` of it), the runners and Fledge runs (none offered to the model), `git-commit` staging changes made outside the file tools, and `git-branch-create --from` an older ref.
- **`shell-exec` refuses foot-guns and says why; it and the language runners start without GitHub or git credentials, bounded and scrubbed; `env -C` and a symlinked `cd` can't leave the root** — [#309](https://github.com/CorvidLabs/Corvidinho/pull/309) (SAFE-21, SAFE-21.a, SAFE-3; REQ-plugins-494, REQ-plugins-495, REQ-plugins-087, REQ-plugins-313, REQ-agent-085): `shell-exec` inherited the full `process.env` (tokens, git credential helpers, the ssh agent), had no timeout, output cap or abort, did not scrub its output, and refused only a `cd` out of the root. The new SAFE-21 check runs before the SAFE-3 clamp and before anything spawns, over the same ground (one walker: dash and bash readings, `eval` / `trap` / `-c` strings, substitutions, and the in-root scripts a command runs), and refuses with exit 2 and `shell-exec refused (SAFE-21): <why>[ (in <script>)]; <what to do instead>` (`data.rule` `SAFE-21`, `family`, `script`), checking four families, most serious first. **download**: a download run as code — piped into a shell, an interpreter (`python`, `perl`, `ruby`, `node`, `php`, `lua`) or `.` reading stdin, also through `env`, `timeout`, `sudo`, `doas` or `xargs`; any shell fed a download, whatever its `-c` runs; `sh -c "$(curl …)"`, `eval "$(curl …)"`, `bash <(curl …)`; a downloaded file then run (`curl -o i.sh … && sh i.sh`, `./i.sh`); a download used as data (`| jq`) is allowed. **delete**: targets outside the worktree, as written or through a symlink, for `rm`, `rmdir`, `unlink`, `shred`, `mv`, `find -delete` / `-exec rm`, `ln -f`, `git worktree remove|move`, `rsync --delete*` / `--remove-source-files`; always `git worktree prune`, a deleting `find -L` / `-follow`, git's deleting subcommands under an outside `-C`, and the worktree's own dir; `$`-expanded targets, `xargs rm` and globs that could match `.` / `..` or cross a symlink fail closed. **secret**: `isSecretPath` files and globs that match one (`cat .en*`), Corvidinho's env and allowlist files and `~/.config/corvidinho`, the gh and git config dirs, `~/.git-credentials`, `~/.gitconfig`, `~/.netrc`, `~/.ssh`, `/proc/*/environ`, object paths like `HEAD:.env`, a tree reader over a dir holding any of these (`grep -r … ~`), credential env vars, `ps e`, `gh auth token`, `git credential*`, the ssh family (`ssh`, `scp`, `rsync` to a remote), and anything that points git or gh back at credentials (`GIT_SSH_COMMAND=…`, `git -c credential.helper=…`, `unset GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL`, and wrappers that build their own env: `env -i`, `exec -c`, `sudo`, `doas`, `su`, `runuser`, `pkexec`). **edit**: `sed -i` / `--in-place`, `perl -i`, `ruby -i`, `awk -i inplace`, `tee` / `sponge` to a file, and every output redirection other than `/dev/null`, stdout, stderr and fd dups, in the typed text (`[[ a > b ]]` and `(( a > b ))` read as redirections; a script's own redirections are a stated residual). The download, delete and secret checks also read the in-root scripts a command runs. `shell-exec` and the node / python / cargo runners now share one child env (the verify-lane scrub plus `withoutGitCredentials`): token, askpass and ssh-agent keys dropped, `GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL=/dev/null`, `GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM=1`, empty command-line `credential.helper`, `http.extraHeader` and `http.https://github.com/.extraHeader`, `GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0`, a key-less `GIT_SSH_COMMAND`, an empty per-process `GH_CONFIG_DIR` (gh logged out) and `CARGO_NET_GIT_FETCH_WITH_CLI=true`, so git there has no owner identity or aliases and pushes, PRs and merges happen only through the checked GitHub tools. `shell-exec` now spawns through `spawnCapped` with the runners' 10-minute timeout (`shell-exec timed out after 600000ms and was killed`), 64 KiB per-stream cap and the run's abort signal, exits 124 / 130 / 127 like them, and its output and the runners' is scrubbed (vendor-key shapes and the literal value of every set secret env var). The SAFE-3 clamp now also checks `env -C` / `--chdir` (clustered or abbreviated) and `sudo -D` / `-R` like `cd`, refuses `env -S` / `sudo -s` strings it cannot read, walks `cd` / `pushd` / `env -C` targets through existing symlinks (fail closed), and refuses an `ln` whose target leads out; its refusal now reads `shell-exec refused (SAFE-3): command would escape the project root (<root>) via cd/pushd/env -C or a symlink to <target>. Stay inside the project cwd.` The model is still never offered the shell, the runners or Fledge runs (`SAFE3_PENDING_TOOLS`; the SAFE-3.a owner grant is later work), so today all of this applies to operator `corvidinho plugins run shell-exec` / `node-exec` / `python-exec` / `cargo-exec`. Residuals: other interpreters' own code (`python3 -c`, `node -e`), tools' own strings (`make`, `npm run`), writers that are not an edit idiom (`cp`, `dd of=`), a recursive reader inside the worktree (the output scrub is the backstop), repo-level git config that embeds a token in a remote URL or includes another file, and `fledge-lanes-run` / `fledge-run`, which keep the verify env.
- **Scheduled runs read and act only on allowlisted repos, even public ones, and a schedule's project can't be a non-allowlisted clone under the bridge root** — [#311](https://github.com/CorvidLabs/Corvidinho/pull/311) (DISCORD-SCHEDULE-3.a, DISCORD-SCHEDULE-3; REQ-plugins-496, REQ-plugins-065, REQ-plugins-493, REQ-plugins-111, REQ-discord-202, REQ-cli-262): every schedule runs with the community stamp, and the community GitHub gate lets any repo through once GitHub confirms it is public (ROLES-CHAT-8), so a schedule and its `delegate` / `council` workers could read any public repo with `github-pr-list`, `github-pr-diff` / `-files`, `github-docs-read` or `github-milestone-list`; and any directory inside the bridge root was a valid schedule project, so a clone of a non-allowlisted repo placed there became the schedule's worktree. A scheduled run is now recognised by its session id (`CORVIDINHO_DISCORD_SESSION_ID` starting `schedule_`, which the scheduler builds from `SCHEDULE_SESSION_PREFIX` and workers inherit). In such a run `checkRepoGateForActingRole` refuses, right after the deny lists and for every role, any repo that fails the GITHUB-6 allowlist, with no visibility lookup and the refusal ending ` — scheduled runs use allowlisted repos only, even public ones (DISCORD-SCHEDULE-3.a)`; that one gate covers every `github-*` command, the review readers and the docs and milestone readers, which exit 3 with zero GitHub requests. A repo that passes still meets the role rules, so a community-stamped schedule still needs an allowlisted repo to be public to read it and never writes. `web-fetch` (dangerous; only when named in `CORVIDINHO_ALLOWLIST`) in a schedule run refuses, on the first hop and on every redirect, before DNS, any `github.com`, `*.github.com`, `githubusercontent.com` or `*.githubusercontent.com` URL that does not name an allowlisted OWNER/REPO (`/<owner>/<repo>/…` on github.com, www, codeload and raw.githubusercontent.com; `/repos/<owner>/<repo>/…` on api.github.com), exit 2 with `web-fetch blocked: refused: <host> is GitHub and this URL is not in an allowlisted repo; scheduled runs read only allowlisted repos, even public ones (DISCORD-SCHEDULE-3.a)`; gists, release assets and LFS objects, avatars, `docs.github.com`, profile, search and `/orgs` pages and other API routes are refused, the allowlist is read once per call and an unreadable one refuses every GitHub hop, and GitHub Pages (`*.github.io`) and third-party mirrors are not gated. `/schedule create` and every tick now resolve the project with the schedule rule: a directory inside a git checkout nested in the bridge root needs that checkout's `origin` on the GitHub allowlist (deny wins; a checkout with no origin is refused: `not authorized: project "<project>" is a repo checkout inside the bridge project root whose origin is not on the GitHub allowlist (scheduled runs use allowlisted repos only)`), so `/schedule create` stores nothing and a stored schedule's tick fails with no agent run and no talk branch; the root's own checkout and plain folders are unchanged, and `/work`, `/session start` and start-up recovery do not apply the rule. Corvidinho's own test preload now clears `CORVIDINHO_DISCORD_SESSION_ID`, so a scheduled run's verify lane on this repo does not trip the gate in its own tests. The shell and runners, which schedules never get, are not gated. No env var, config key, command, table or schema change.

### Ops

- Package version **0.0.35** — restart the Discord bridge, `corvidinho daemon` and `github watch` after update (the bridge re-registers its slash set on ready, which adds `/admin people forget`; still nine top-level commands). **Schema stays v13**: `SCHEMA_VERSION` in `src/store/db.ts` is 13, unchanged since 0.0.34, so no migration runs and no table, column or index is added; `SCRUB_RULES_VERSION` stays 3, so there is no re-scrub pass. #301 only writes new value shapes into existing columns: `forget_requests.requester_user_id` (`github:<numeric id>:<login>`, `admin:<owner Discord id>`), `forget_requests.origin_channel_id` (`github:<owner/repo>#<n>`) and `github-id:<n>` entries in `conversation_threads.participants`. Talk worktrees gain a `corvidinho-verified` marker file in their git dir (#308), never in the working tree. v0.0.35 gets its tag and Release from CI when this bump lands on main; the bridge's restart post links `…/releases/tag/v0.0.35` (#305), which 404s until release.yml has created that Release.
- New and changed operator surface: allowlist-file key `[owner] github_id` (optional, no default — unset means the owner is not recognised on GitHub; 1 to 20 digits, quoted or bare in TOML, a string or a safe integer above 0 in JSON, leading zeros dropped, `0` is not an id; an invalid value is ignored and `corvidinho doctor`'s `owner` line turns `[info]` with `owner github_id is not a numeric GitHub user id (ignored — the owner is not recognised on GitHub until fixed)` (in a JSON file, a number that is not a safe integer above 0 reads `owner github_id in the JSON allowlist file is not a numeric GitHub user id (ignored)` instead); there is no env twin, and env still overrides the other owner fields; read when the bridge and `github watch` start, so restart them after setting it; `gh api users/<login> --jq .id` prints the id, as `allowlist.example.toml` now says). `[people.<id>] github_ids` (existing, read live) is now the only GitHub match; `github_logins`, `[owner] github_login` and `CORVIDINHO_OWNER_GITHUB_LOGIN` are labels (the owner's login is still what SAFE-13 and summary comments @mention). Removed: the CLI flag `--no-verify` (exit 1 on any command) and the project `fledge.toml` key `[corvidinho] verify_before_complete` (ignored, with `[warn] verify-gate`). New owner-only slash subcommand `/admin people forget person:<id>` (SAFE-5 `admin-people-forget`); `/admin people link … github:<login>` now makes one GitHub API call from the bridge (`GET /users/{login}`, with `GITHUB_TOKEN` / `GH_TOKEN` when set, anonymous otherwise, 10 s timeout). New doctor lines `[warn] people-github` and `[warn] verify-gate`, which never fail doctor, so the updater's doctor step does not roll back on them. New `privateReplies` field on the `task run` json / ndjson result frame (at most 5, 6000 characters each). New log lines: `[scheduler] SAFE-13: schedule <id> not run: …; pausing it`, `[watch] forget-me …`, `[watch] forget-me reply failed …`, `[watch] forget outcome … posted …`, `[watch] forget outcome post failed …`, `[watch] forget request <id>: thread not reachable; the outcome was not posted`, `[watch] forget outcomes read failed: …`, `[watch] forget outcomes failed …` and `[discord] /admin people forget: card delivery failed: …`. No new env var. The schedule repo gate (#311) has no switch and uses the existing GitHub allowlist (`[github] repos` / `orgs` / `deny_repos` / `deny_orgs`, `CORVIDINHO_GITHUB_ALLOW_REPOS` / `_ALLOW_ORGS` / `_DENY_REPOS` / `_DENY_ORGS`).
- GitHub identity (#303) needs operator action: a person declared only by `github_logins` (no `github_ids`) still loads and still matches on Discord, but on GitHub now reads as undeclared community — their WATCH block says `declared_person: none` (once anyone besides the owner is declared, or when their login is someone's label), they get no personal memory in WATCH runs (only `memory-recall --project` of the thread's repo, read-only) and save nothing there, and a GitHub "forget me" from them gets the `I can't confirm this GitHub account …` reply and no card. The owner with `[owner] github_login` but no `github_id` likewise: their comments get the `declared_person: none` block instead of `role: owner`, WATCH runs get no owner memory, and their titles and bodies are now checked by the SAFE-13 detector like anyone's (they were already fenced). `corvidinho doctor` names who is affected in `[warn] people-github` (person ids only). Fix it by setting `[owner] github_id`, adding `github_ids`, or re-running `/admin people link person:<id> github:<login>` for each linked login (it adds the looked-up id). A `[people.<id>]` entry whose `github_ids` holds `0` (the `allowlist.example.toml` placeholder) was accepted by 0.0.34 and is now skipped whole, so that person also loses Discord recognition and their role until the `0` is removed or replaced; `/admin people list` shows it as `person "<id>": a github id is not a numeric GitHub user id — skipped` (and `/admin config show` counts the problem). The WATCH user allowlist (`[github] users`), the assignment / review-request actor gate and the owner's @mention still use logins, so a re-registered allowlisted login can still start a WATCH run, now as community.
- Roles and verify: every non-owner not declared `role = "team"` now gets `not authorized` on `/work`; with nobody declared, only the owner can use it, so declare team members first: `role = "team"` with their `discord_ids` in `[people.<id>]`, or `/admin people add`, `link … discord:` and then `role … role:team` (#300). `--no-verify` anywhere on a `corvidinho` command line (outside `--task` text and after `plugins run <name> --`) now exits 1 before anything runs — drop it from scripts and aliases; a project `fledge.toml` with `verify_before_complete = false` now verifies on every surface — remove the key to clear the doctor warning (#308). Talk worktrees created before this update have no `corvidinho-verified` marker, so the first run in each existing talk verifies every edit since that talk started (from its merge-base), and chat in a talk whose last run did not end `done` runs the verify lane on every turn that finds any edit since the talk started, until one ends `done`; a project whose lane is broken for unrelated reasons keeps those turns ending `failed`, by design.
- Schedules (#307, #311): a schedule whose project is a directory in a git checkout nested inside the bridge root now needs that checkout's `origin` on the GitHub allowlist; an existing one fails at its next tick with the REQ-discord-353 stuck ask and is auto-paused after 5 failed runs in a row (`Paused after 5 failed runs in a row. …`) — allowlist the repo or `/schedule delete` it. Schedule runs (and their `delegate` / `council` workers) that read public repos off the allowlist, through the GitHub tools or `web-fetch` on GitHub hosts, now get refusals; add those repos to `[github] repos` / `orgs` if the schedule needs them (reads still need the repo to be public, since schedules run as community). A schedule whose creator is not the owner and whose stored name, description or prompt trips the SAFE-13 detector is paused at its next tick with one stuck ask pinging the owner; resuming it only repeats the refusal at the next tick, and `/schedule delete` removes it. Other non-owner schedules (stored while admin lists still granted ADMIN, or by someone who is no longer the owner) now run with their name and prompt fenced as `UNTRUSTED_DATA`.
- Shell (#309): operator `corvidinho plugins run shell-exec -- …` now refuses (exit 2, `shell-exec refused (SAFE-21): …; <what to do instead>`) output redirections to files (`> file`, `>> file`, `tee file`), `sed -i` and other in-place edits, `curl … | sh` and other downloads run as code, deletes outside the worktree, reads of `.env`, credential stores and `~/.ssh`, and `ssh` / `scp` / remote `rsync`; `2>&1` and `> /dev/null` still work. The SAFE-3 clamp also now refuses (exit 2, `shell-exec refused (SAFE-3): command would escape the project root (…) via cd/pushd/env -C or a symlink to …`) an `env -C` / `--chdir` or `sudo -D` / `-R` directory outside the root, a `cd` through an existing symlink that leads out, an `ln` whose target leads out, and `env -S` / `sudo -s` strings it cannot read. `shell-exec` also no longer gets the full process env: it now runs with the runners' verify-lane env (no LLM API keys, `DISCORD_*` config, audit key or `CORVIDINHO_ACTING_*` identity). The shell and the node / python / cargo runners now run with no GitHub token, no global or system git config (no user identity or aliases), no credential helper, a key-less ssh and gh logged out, so `git push`, `gh pr create` or a commit that needs the global identity fail there; use the typed tools or run the command outside `shell-exec`. `shell-exec` output past 64 KiB per stream is cut with a note, and a command running over 10 minutes is killed (exit 124). The model still never gets the shell.
- Privacy and forget (#302, #301): `memory-profile`, private notes and the owner's `--person` view now arrive by DM, so the person (or the owner) must share a server with the bot and accept DMs from its members, the same prerequisite as the forget card; otherwise the channel gets the `… couldn't DM it to you …` note and the text is shown nowhere. `memory-profile` is now refused in schedule runs and in WATCH runs, since neither has a private place to show it; private notes and `--person` were already refused there. A GitHub "forget me" goes through the usual WATCH repo and user allowlist gates first, so the asker's login must be on `[github] users` (default-deny) on an allowlisted repo, or the comment is refused quietly with no reply and no card; it also needs `github watch` and the bridge on the same `CORVIDINHO_DATA_DIR` (and, with a keyed audit chain, `CORVIDINHO_AUDIT_HMAC_KEY` in both — without it the watch answers `I couldn't record your forget request right now …`), a running bridge to DM the card and to close an unanswered ask as a no after 24 h (the watch posts the no-answer outcome only once the bridge's pass, or a new ask from that person, has closed it), and the repo still allowlisted for the outcome comment; the watch process posts the replies and outcomes. An `/admin people forget` card goes out through the bridge's single-flight delivery pass, so it can wait for the next scheduler tick (up to 60 s) if a pass is already running.
- Other behaviour operators will see: the bridge's restart post in the `/announce` channel is the one-line `Back online and running **v0.0.35** …` note linking the release notes (#305); a team or community member's Answer-form text and picked Choose labels reach the run fenced as `UNTRUSTED_DATA`, and a flagged Answer-form submit gets the ephemeral SAFE-13 refusal with an owner ping and an `injection-suspected` audit row instead of a run (#299, #310); a declared team member allowlisted only through a Discord role now resumes a pick or Answer-form answer as team, as their chat does, instead of community (#299); a press on an option id the ask does not have answers `that choice expired` for everyone, the owner included (#310); ask questions and labels that held a secret show `[redacted:<kind>]` on buttons, stubs, forms and stored asks (#306); the file tools, `git-commit` deletions and `discord-send-file` refuse `.fledge/` like `fledge.toml` (#304).
- Rollback to 0.0.34: the schema is the same v13, so 0.0.34 opens the DB with no migration and ignores the `corvidinho-verified` markers. It ignores `[owner] github_id` (its owner parser reads only `discord_id`, `github_login` and `display`) and matches GitHub logins again, loads a `[people.<id>]` entry with `github_ids = ["0"]` again, lets community run the read-only `/work` again, honours `--no-verify` and `verify_before_complete` again, stops protecting `.fledge/`, drops the SAFE-21 refusals, gives `shell-exec` the full environment again with no timeout, output cap or scrub, returns the owner's global git config and ssh agent to the node / python / cargo runners, drops the SAFE-3 `env -C` / `sudo -D` / symlink / `ln` checks, returns private reads to the model, reads public repos and nested non-allowlisted checkouts from schedules again, runs a non-owner's schedule prompt unfenced and unscanned, passes a non-owner's Answer-form text (unscanned) and picked labels to the run unfenced, resumes a pick on an unknown option id with that raw id, cuts ask questions and labels before scrubbing them, and posts the old `bridge live` note with CHANGELOG bullets. Schedules #307 paused stay paused. Decide open GitHub and `/admin` forget asks before rolling back: 0.0.34 reads their stored requester (`github:<id>:<login>` / `admin:<owner id>`) as a Discord user id, so its card shows it as a mention it cannot resolve, it cannot tell the asker, and nothing is posted on the GitHub thread.

## 0.0.34

### Knows everyone (M1)

- **The owner declares who's who, and Corvidinho recognises each declared person on Discord and GitHub by stable ids** — [#271](https://github.com/CorvidLabs/Corvidinho/pull/271) (IDENTITY-13, IDENTITY-14, IDENTITY-6, IDENTITY-7, ADMIN-3.a; REQ-discord-036, REQ-watch-036, REQ-discord-043, REQ-discord-446): Corvidinho knew only the configured owner; everyone else was a bare Discord id or GitHub login. The allowlist file the process loaded (the one `[owner]` comes from; a `people` object in a JSON file) now takes `[people.<id>]` sections with `display`, `nicknames`, `discord_ids`, `github_logins` and `github_ids` (singular spellings are read too). A person id is a lowercase slug of up to 32 letters, digits, `-` or `_`; `owner` is reserved. People are matched only on Discord user ids, GitHub numeric ids and GitHub logins, never on a display name or nickname; a login match is dropped when GitHub reports a numeric id and the person declared other `github_ids` (with no `github_ids` declared, the login alone matches), and an id declared for two people matches nobody. An entry with any unreadable value is skipped whole and reported by person id and key, never by account id. The list is re-read on every use, so edits apply without a restart. The configured owner is always a person (the declared holder of the owner's Discord id, else a built-in `owner` entry). On Discord, chat, button-pick resumes, `/session start` and `/work` add `declared_person`, the declared `display_name` (which now wins over the Discord name), `nicknames` and `github` to the IDENTITY-4 block, matched on the acting Discord id only; once anyone is declared, an undeclared speaker other than the owner is marked `declared_person: none`, and with nobody declared (or for an owner not declared under `[people]`) #271 leaves the block as it was (the SAFE-11 name cleaning and `name_clash` line from #295 below still apply). On GitHub, WATCH events now carry the commenter's numeric id, and the run prompt carries a `[Corvidinho acting GitHub user …]` block (after any #292 memory blocks) for a declared commenter and for the `[owner]` login (`role: owner`, which still gets no ADMIN tools in a GitHub run); once anyone is declared, an undeclared commenter's block says `declared_person: none`. New owner-only `/admin people list|add|link|unlink|remove` (still nine top-level slash commands) is the only writer besides the VM file: each change writes a SAFE-5 `admin-people-<op>` row (`started` before the atomic write, then `ok` / `error`; refusals and non-owners `denied`), and with no audit trail it is refused and nothing is written. A re-read safety net refuses any rewrite that would touch allow/deny lists, `[owner]`, another person or another section, TOML edits keep the person's header, comments and unread keys, and linking an id already held by someone else (including the `[owner]` login) is refused. `/admin config show` counts declared people. A WATCH process started without an allowlist file needs a restart to see one created later.
- **Owner, team and community roles gate every tool, re-checked in the tool layer on every call** — [#285](https://github.com/CorvidLabs/Corvidinho/pull/285) (IDENTITY-8, IDENTITY-9, IDENTITY-10, IDENTITY-11, IDENTITY-12, ADMIN-3.b, ROLES-CHAT-8.a; REQ-plugins-065, REQ-plugins-066, REQ-agent-065, REQ-discord-065, REQ-discord-043, REQ-discord-088, REQ-agent-501): the tool gate had two tiers, ADMIN (the owner) and everyone else. Each `[people.<id>]` entry now takes `role = "team"` or `role = "community"`; no `role` key is community, the configured owner is always owner, `role = "owner"` on anyone else grants nothing (community, with a reported problem), and a list or unknown value makes the entry unreadable and skipped whole. The role is resolved again at every tool call from the owner config and the people list read fresh, matched on the Discord id only; `runPlugin` and the task-run catalog share one rule, and an allowlist file that cannot be read or parsed (any error while resolving) reads as community. The Discord spawn stamps `CORVIDINHO_ACTING_ROLE` (and `CORVIDINHO_ACTING_WORK_TASK=1` for `/work`) as a per-surface cap that can only lower the role; chat, button picks, `/session start` and `/work` spawn with the speaker's role, while WATCH, schedules and `delegate` / `council` workers are community. Owner is today's ADMIN. Team gets the read tools, `github-issue-comment` and `github-pr-review` (still behind SAFE-1, only on GITHUB-6-allowlisted repos, and a team review with `--event APPROVE` or `REQUEST_CHANGES` is refused exit 2, `Denied: github-pr-review --event … is not allowed for your role (IDENTITY-10: team reviews post as COMMENT).`), and `files-write` / `files-edit` only in their own `/work` run (SAFE-2 applies and secret-looking paths are refused); their `/work` opens the draft PR like the owner's, with the role checked again after the run so a member demoted meanwhile gets no PR. Memory store and recall stay their own; forget and override stay owner-only; no shell, runners, git writes, other GitHub writes, Discord posts, `web-fetch`, `delegate` or `council`. Community keeps today's non-ADMIN catalog with no mutating tool. Team reads work on allowlisted or confirmed-public repos, team writes only on allowlisted ones, community writes are refused, and deny lists still come first. Two new read-only tools serve ROLES-CHAT-8.a behind the acting role's repo gate: `github-docs-read` (the README by default, a root `STATUS*` / `CHANGELOG*` file, or anything under `docs/`, where a directory lists up to 200 entries; any other path is refused before GitHub is called; text scrubbed, capped at 64 KiB and labelled untrusted; secret-looking doc paths are refused and hidden for every non-owner) and `github-milestone-list`. New owner-only `/admin people role person:<id> role:<team|community>` writes the SAFE-5 `admin-people-role` row before the atomic write, fails closed without an audit trail, and refuses the owner role, unknown roles, undeclared people and the owner's own person; `/admin people list` shows each role and `/admin config show` counts them. With no role stamp (the local CLI, older callers) the old ADMIN bit behaves exactly as before.
- **Each declared person has one memory profile, each project has shared memory, private notes stay private, and anyone can ask to be forgotten on an owner Approve/Deny card** — [#291](https://github.com/CorvidLabs/Corvidinho/pull/291) (MEMORY-5, MEMORY-6, MEMORY-7, MEMORY-ACL-6; REQ-discord-101, REQ-plugins-101, REQ-agent-101, REQ-discord-021, REQ-plugins-010): memory was keyed only by Discord id. A declared person's memory is now one profile, scope `person:<id>`, reached from every Discord id linked to them; rows stored under those ids before they were declared are still read, and undeclared users keep their Discord-id scope. New categories `project`, `preference`, `decision`, `ask`, `approval` and `private`; the new `memory-profile` shows the person's role (from the people list), projects, preferences and history newest first, and only counts private notes. Project memory, scope `project:<key>`, is keyed by the `origin` remote's owner/repo, lowercased with any URL credentials dropped (without an `origin`, the main checkout's real path, so every talk worktree shares it); the owner, team and the local CLI use it with `memory-store --project` / `memory-recall --project`, owner and team chat, button-pick and `/work` runs get it as a second prompt block, and community, schedules and workers cannot read or write it (WATCH reads it only as below). Only the owner may read another person's memory with `--person`; anyone else gets a plain `not authorized` whether or not that person exists. Private notes (`--category private`) are never injected into a prompt and come back only when that person or the owner asks for them with `memory-recall --category private` in a Discord conversation (a run with a reply channel: chat, button picks, `/session start`, `/work`), never in a schedule or other run (`refused: private notes are shown only in a conversation with that person or the owner, never in a schedule or other run (MEMORY-7)`). The new `memory-forget-me` records the acting person's ask (audited, one open ask per person, deletes nothing; refused when no owner is configured). The bridge DMs the owner an Approve/Deny card on every scheduler tick and right after each chat message, naming who asked, how many memories Approve would delete (never their content), what is kept and when it lapses. Only the owner's press counts, re-checked at press time. Approve writes the SAFE-5 `memory-forget-approve` `started` row first (no row, nothing deleted), then one transaction closes the ask and deletes every memory row and stored session turn of that person, and the running bridge drops the session threads it holds for them; the press is answered first, then the asker is told by DM (or in the allowlisted conversation they asked in if the DM fails, retried for about a day). Deny, no answer within 24 h, or a late press is a no and deletes nothing. Kept: the people-list entry, project memory, what others stored, schedules, `/work` records, the sessions themselves, the ask row and the audit trail. Asks live in the new `forget_requests` table (added by the v12 migration; with #296 the release ships schema v13; ids, status and times only, nothing to scrub). No new config key, env var or slash command.
- **Memory works on GitHub for the commenter's declared person, recall is ranked, and the agent searches memory before saying it doesn't know** — [#292](https://github.com/CorvidLabs/Corvidinho/pull/292) (MEMORY-8, MEMORY-9; REQ-watch-067, REQ-plugins-067, REQ-agent-067, REQ-discord-067, REQ-watch-008): WATCH runs refused memory, and `memory-recall --query` was a plain match. The WATCH spawn now passes the commenter's GitHub login and numeric id and the thread's repo as `CORVIDINHO_ACTING_GITHUB_LOGIN` / `_ID` / `_REPO`, taken from the GitHub API event, never the comment text (the Discord spawn always clears them; delegate workers drop every `CORVIDINHO_ACTING_*` key). The memory plugins resolve them against the people list at every call: a declared commenter saves to and reads their own `person:<id>` profile, the same one they have on Discord; the owner, while not declared under `[people]`, keeps their Discord-id memory; an undeclared commenter can only read the thread repo's project memory with `memory-recall --project` and saves nothing. On GitHub, project memory is read-only for everyone, and `--person`, private notes and `memory-forget-me` are refused (threads are public). `memory-recall --query` is now a ranked search: question words dropped, light stemming, idf-weighted term hits, key hits counted double, a whole-query bonus, times a recency weight with a 30-day half-life that never drops below 3/4, over at most 500 candidates (no FTS table, no schema change). Before the model runs, memory is searched for the incoming message and the relevant rows go first, then the newest: the Discord chat and button-pick inject, the owner's and team's `/work` project block (searched for the description) and, new, the WATCH poller for each comment (the commenter's profile and the repo's project memory, logged as `[watch] memory inject: N recalled for @login`, with ` (project only)` for an undeclared commenter). A loop guard runs when a final reply says it doesn't know and the person's own or the project's memory was not searched in that attempt: it runs only the missing `memory-recall --query` / `--project --query` itself through the normal plugin gates, keeps the reply when nothing is found (no extra model call), and otherwise gives the facts back to the model once without using a tool round. Recall results are now ordered by relevance first.
- **One editable `persona.md` in corvid-agent's voice is loaded into every run on every surface, and the rules still win** — [#274](https://github.com/CorvidLabs/Corvidinho/pull/274) (PERSONA-1, PERSONA-2, PERSONA-3; REQ-agent-069): runs had no persona. `persona.md` at the root of Corvidinho's own checkout (the one whose `src/cli.ts` runs the task, never the project a run works in) holds the voice: warm, direct, with emoji, never a flat changelog. `createTaskExecute` loads it once per run, so every surface (chat, slash commands, `/work`, schedules, WATCH, the CLI `task run`, delegate and council workers) reads it fresh each turn. In a git checkout only the copy committed at `HEAD` loads, capped at 8 KiB with a marker; symlinks out of the checkout, binary and non-UTF-8 files are refused, the text is SAFE-6 scrubbed and a `</persona` close tag is escaped. The system prompt order is the persona block (under a header saying it is tone only, not facts, tools or permissions), then "You are Corvidinho…", then the PERSONA-3 rules (one message per turn, though a DISCORD-17 attachment is fine; no spam; no unchecked claims) and the other rules, then project instructions. A missing, empty, refused, truncated or uncommitted-edit file never stops a run: it emits one `Persona: persona.md …` note naming only the file, which bridges and WATCH do not post. No flag, env var or config key.
- **A nightly SQLite backup goes to a directory the owner sets, a weekly restore test checks it, and `corvidinho backup restore` puts one back** — [#281](https://github.com/CorvidLabs/Corvidinho/pull/281) (OPS-1, OPS-2; REQ-cli-680, REQ-discord-680): there was no backup. New optional `CORVIDINHO_BACKUP_DIR` must be an absolute local directory outside any git work tree (a symlink into one is refused too); unset means no backup, as before. From the existing scheduler tick of the bridge and `corvidinho daemon`, at the first tick at or after 03:00 local time, the night is claimed in SQLite (a bridge and a daemon on one data dir back up once, and the daemon backs up even on a tick whose allowlist file fails to load); `VACUUM INTO` writes a consistent copy under umask 077, re-scrubbed first if the scrub rules tightened, which is checked (integrity_check, schema version, tables, row counts), fsynced and renamed to `corvidinho-<UTC>Z.db` (mode 0600). The newest 7 are kept; other files are never touched except a crashed run's `.corvidinho-*.db.tmp` older than an hour. Weekly, and every night while it is failing, the newest snapshot is restored into a temp dir with the restore code, checked against the row counts recorded when it was taken, and deleted. Every run is logged (daemon JSON events `backup.ok` / `backup.failed` / `restore_test.ok` / `restore_test.failed` / `restore_test.skipped`, bridge `[backup] <event> {…}` lines); the first failure of a streak records an owner notice that the bridge's next tick posts once to the `/announce` channel pinging only the owner, as fixed text (`⚠️ The nightly backup failed (…)` / `⚠️ The restore test failed (…)`, no host path or error), handed back and retried every tick when the post does not go out (a bridge stopping mid-post waits 3 s, then hands it back); while it cannot post, the bridge logs `[backup] backup.owner_not_told {…}` (or `restore_test.owner_not_told`) once, then `….owner_told` when the notice goes out. A job whose process died is recorded as that job's failure (`interrupted: true`) and told. New `corvidinho backup list` and `corvidinho backup restore <snapshot> <target> [--force]` (exit 1, `restore refused: …`; both read `CORVIDINHO_BACKUP_DIR` from the shell that runs them and exit 1 with `CORVIDINHO_BACKUP_DIR is not set …` when it is unset, and `<snapshot>` is a file name from `backup list`, not a path): a target any process holds open (a `/proc` fd scan, plus a live `daemon.lock` for `corvidinho.db`) is refused even with `--force`, any other existing target needs `--force`, and a snapshot with a schema newer than the build is refused. `corvidinho doctor` gains a `backup` line, `[warn]` when off, unusable, failing or when no `/announce` channel is set, never failing doctor. State lives in `schema_meta` `ops_*` keys; no table or schema version.

### Talk anywhere (M2)

- **`discord-send-file` attaches files and images to replies in the conversation's own channel** — [#270](https://github.com/CorvidLabs/Corvidinho/pull/270) (DISCORD-17, DISCORD-5, DISCORD-8, SAFE-1, SAFE-2, SAFE-5, SAFE-6, ROLES-CHAT-3; REQ-discord-476, REQ-agent-476): the agent could not attach anything and told people so. New plugin `discord-send-file` (dangerous, mutating, minTier 1) must be named in `CORVIDINHO_ALLOWLIST` like `discord-post-message`, writes a SAFE-5 `started` row before every call it runs and an `ok` or `error` row after it (a refusal inside the tool, such as a wrong channel or an oversize file, is an `error` row; a SAFE-1 denial writes `denied`), and is refused by ROLES-CHAT-3 in non-owner, WATCH and schedule runs. It attaches only in the channel the bridge sets per spawn (`CORVIDINHO_DISCORD_REPLY_CHANNEL_ID`, plus `CORVIDINHO_DISCORD_REPLY_PARENT_CHANNEL_ID` in a thread; always overwritten, empty when unset, never inherited): chat, reply-continue, thread and ask-button runs pass the conversation's channel, `/session start` and `/work` the command's channel, schedules nothing. A `--channel` / `-c` / `--channel-id` argument, a run with no conversation channel, or one with no acting user is refused (exit 3, `… Nothing was sent.`). Gates, in order: the channel allowlist, then the path, type and size checks on the file (or the diff), then the DISCORD-8 check that the acting user has View Channel, Send Messages **and Attach Files** in the channel (fails closed when it cannot run, naming Server Members Intent). At most 8 MB (exit 2); a Discord 413 or code 40005 is reported as the server's lower limit and not retried. Images must be PNG, JPEG, GIF or WebP with magic bytes matching the extension; text must be UTF-8 `.txt`, `.log`, `.md`, `.diff`, `.patch`, `.json` or `.csv`. Text, diffs and the caption are secret-scrubbed (vendor-key shapes and the literal values of secret env vars, now shared with `formatErrorLine` as `redactSecretEnvValues`), and the caption parses no mentions. SAFE-2 protected infra, anything under `.specsync`, secret paths and paths that leave the project are refused, on the path as given and where it resolves. `--git-diff [--staged]` attaches the talk worktree's diff as `changes.diff` / `staged.diff` (secret paths excluded, an empty diff refused). `CORVIDINHO_DISCORD_DRY_RUN=1` posts nothing. The model is told it can attach (never to say it can't) only when the tool is offered and the run has a conversation channel. Images are not scrubbed or EXIF-stripped, and one file goes per call.
- **`discord-send-file` serves a thread allowlisted by its own id, reads the file once within the cap, and refuses a file swapped for a link after its checks** — [#289](https://github.com/CorvidLabs/Corvidinho/pull/289) (DISCORD-17, SAFE-2; REQ-discord-476): the plugin gated a thread only through its parent, so it refused every attach in a thread allowlisted by its own id even though the router served it and offered the tool; and it ran `statSync` then `readFileSync`, which followed links and read a grown file whole. The conversation now goes through the router's own `isMonitoredConversation` on the bridge's channel set: a thread passes when it or its parent is listed, and a deny on either still wins (`is denied` / `not allowlisted` as before). The file is read from one descriptor opened with `O_NOFOLLOW`, must be a regular file, and at most 8 MB + 1 byte is ever read, so a file over 8 MB, or one that grew past it, is refused with the upload-limit error without being read whole. The opened file's own path is read back from `/proc/self/fd` and checked again, so a file or folder replaced by a link after the path checks is refused (`… became a symlink after it was checked …` / `… now opens a protected, secret or outside path …`), and if `/proc` is not mounted every file is refused (`could not confirm which file … opened`). `docs/DISCORD-GO-LIVE.md` step 4 now lists **Attach Files** for the bot invite.
- **Free-text asks get an Answer button that opens a private form; replying still works** — [#294](https://github.com/CorvidLabs/Corvidinho/pull/294) (DISCORD-ASK-4.a; REQ-discord-548): a clarify or stuck ask whose choices could not be listed was answered only by a public reply. Its post (the quoted question, footer kept, saved as the ask's `stubMessageId`) now carries one **Answer** button and the hint `Press **Answer** to answer privately, or reply to this message.`, also on follow-up asks from a resumed pick or form, thin-reply restatements while the ask is live, and `/work` and `/session start` free-text answers. The requester's press opens a Discord modal (`cvask:answer:<askId>`, title `Answer privately`, label `Your answer` with up to the first 100 characters of the question, scrubbed, as its description, one required paragraph input of at most 1500 characters); the press itself posts and runs nothing. The submit goes through the same gates as a button press (channel, actor, mute and rate limit, not-yours or already answered, expiry), each refusal ephemeral with no run and the ask kept open. The typed text is SAFE-6 scrubbed, trimmed and capped and never posted. Unlike a reply in the channel, it is not put in the SAFE-12 `UNTRUSTED_DATA` fence and not checked by the SAFE-13 detector (#295), whoever the requester is, so a team or community requester's private answer reaches the model as written. A thin or blank answer (`ok`, `sure`, emoji, whitespace) is restated privately with the button again and runs nothing; `cancel` / `never mind` drops every open ask of the session with the private `Got it — cancelled. Ready when you have a real answer.`; any other answer clears the ask, shows the ephemeral `Got it — working on it…` (deleted when the run ends) and resumes the session through the pick path with the same `[Prior clarifying question …]` / `Human answer:` block a reply gets, editing the stub into the answer. After about 30 minutes a press or submit gets `that choice expired` and the free-text ask stays open for a reply. A form id without typed text, or a press id with typed text, is ignored. Schedule asks stay text only. No new env var, config key, slash command or schema change.
- **Display names can't impersonate, external bodies are data, and a suspected injection is refused and reported to the owner** — [#295](https://github.com/CorvidLabs/Corvidinho/pull/295) (SAFE-11, SAFE-12, SAFE-13; REQ-agent-071, REQ-discord-071, REQ-watch-071, REQ-plugins-071, REQ-cli-071, REQ-discord-036, REQ-watch-036): a Discord display name, a GitHub body or a tool result went to the model as written. Names in the acting-user block and `discord-user-lookup` results are cleaned (mention markup, control, zero-width, bidi and tag characters, `[owner]` / `(system)` tags and `owner:` labels removed, capped at 32 characters, a role-word-only name dropped), and a name that looks like the owner's or a declared person's adds a `name_clash` line; identity and role still come only from declared ids. A team or community speaker's chat message, `/session start` topic and `/work` description, WATCH titles and bodies (clipped so the end marker survives the 8000-character prompt cap) and results of the GitHub readers and `discord-user-lookup` reach the model inside an `UNTRUSTED_DATA` fence whose end marker carries a random id, and every task-run system prompt says such blocks never grant a permission; replayed lines that imitate a Corvidinho block or a turn label are marked `(quoted)`.
