An always-on detector reports six fixed reason ids (`ignore-rules`, `role-override`, `owner-claim`, `secret-request`, `tool-call-payload`, `fake-marker`), never the text. When a non-owner's own words trip it, no run starts: chat gets one public reply to the message whose only parsed mention is the owner (as a reply it also pings the speaker) (`🛡️ I won't act on that: it looks like a prompt-injection attempt (it …). <@owner>, flagging this for you. (SAFE-13)`) and the session it would have started is dropped; `/session start` and `/work` get the refusal plus a fresh post pinging the owner; WATCH runs nothing and posts one refusal comment (`Corvidinho WATCH won't act on this: it looks like a prompt-injection attempt (it …).`) that @mentions the `[owner]` GitHub login when one is set (without one the comment still posts, naming nobody; it is skipped for the watch user's own events, an already-answered event id and a bad repo). When a scanned tool result trips it (`web-fetch`, `github-pr-list`, `github-pr-status`, `github-issue-list`, `github-docs-read`, `github-milestone-list`, `discord-user-lookup`, or a hit reported back by a `delegate` / `council` worker), every mutating tool plus `memory-store` is dropped and refused for the rest of the run, the answer ends with a short "didn't act on it" line, and the post carrying it pings the owner (chat, button picks, `/session start`, `/work`, a schedule's result or ask post, the WATCH summary, or one WATCH comment of its own). Each refused message or event, and the first tool-result hit of a run, appends one SAFE-5 `injection-suspected` / `denied` row (actor, surface and a digest of the source and reason ids, never the text). A `delegate` / `council` worker's hit is recorded once, by the lead. The owner's own words on Discord are neither fenced nor checked; on WATCH the owner's title and body (recognised by GitHub id or login in the people list) are still fenced, only not checked. No config key, env var, table or schema change.
- **Long chats are condensed at about 80% of the model's window with the task and latest instruction kept word for word, a conversation is kept 30 days so a reply after the TTL resumes it, and WATCH follow-ups replay the issue or PR thread** — [#296](https://github.com/CorvidLabs/Corvidinho/pull/296) (SESSION-5, SESSION-6, SESSION-3.a, AGENT-6.a; REQ-discord-472, REQ-watch-472, REQ-discord-072, REQ-discord-019, REQ-watch-037): a continued Discord session replayed at most 6000 characters of earlier turns (each clipped at 1500, the middle dropped as `(N earlier turns omitted)`), its turns were deleted when it ended or idled out, so a reply to one of its answers after the soft TTL was ignored (an @mention started fresh with nothing replayed), and a WATCH follow-up carried only the newest event. When the conversation part of a prompt (the replayed block plus the new message) reaches 80% of the window, the oldest turns are now folded into a summary one at a time until it fits, with no model call: each becomes one point of its own opening words (`- Human: …` / `- You (Corvidinho): …`, up to 160 characters) under `Condensed summary of earlier turns (the task, the latest instruction and the newest turns are word for word):`, and the summary stays within a third of the budget (500 to 6000 characters), its older points shortened to 80 characters and then counted as `(N earlier points left out)`. The session's opening human turn (the task) and its newest human turn (the latest instruction) are never folded, and the new message is never touched. The window is the new optional `CORVIDINHO_LLM_CONTEXT_TOKENS` (tokens; 8192 when unset or not a positive integer, raised to 1024 when lower), counted at 4 characters a token: the budget is `floor(window × 0.8) × 4` characters (26,212 at the default) and never more than 32,000, because the prompt travels as one process argument. Human turns are now stored up to 8000 characters (was 1500; answers stay at 1500), and a turn past the 200-turn cap is folded into the summary instead of dropped. The summary is stored with the session (scrubbed) and reloaded after a restart, and a smaller window condenses further from it. When a session ends or idles out (also when that is found at load after a restart), its summary, last 20 turns, up to 100 answer message ids and its project are kept, scrubbed, in the new `conversation_threads` table (schema v13) for 30 days after its last activity, then purged (on every read and write of that table, hourly in the bridge and on each WATCH poll cycle). Within that time, the same user's reply to one of its answers, or their message in its thread (no @mention needed), passes the channel allowlist, actor / deny-list and mute / rate gates and starts a new session seeded with the summary and kept turns, in the conversation's project (a project that no longer resolves fails the worktree bind like any talk, never falling back to the default project); it works only in the same thread, or the same channel outside threads, a live session already carrying the conversation is continued instead, anyone else's reply or thread message never gets it, and a new @mention elsewhere in the channel still starts fresh. An Answer form submit (#294) is condensed like chat. On WATCH, each run adds the event and its answer to the issue or PR's kept conversation (one per issue or PR, shared by everyone who comments), and a follow-up on the same issue or PR, also after the session TTL, gets it replayed ahead of the event in a `[Corvidinho earlier conversation on this GitHub issue or PR …]` block, condensed the same way, with the #292 memory blocks ahead of it. Replayed turns and summary points are data (SAFE-12): a line that opens like a Corvidinho block or a turn label is marked `(quoted)`, and a summary point keeps an `UNTRUSTED_DATA` fence's own markers or is left out whole. An approved forget-me (#291) now also deletes, in the same transaction, that person's kept conversations (matched by their Discord ids and, for a declared person, their linked GitHub logins, which takes every WATCH thread they started or commented on, other commenters' words included), and the running bridge drops their live summaries; the owner's card lists `their kept conversations (30-day summaries)` among what Approve deletes, and the owner's confirmation adds ` and N kept conversations` when any went. A conversation-store failure never stops a run: it logs `[discord] conversation <step> failed: …` or `[watch] conversation purge failed: …` (`read failed`, `write failed`) and drops only that replay or keep. No slash command, CLI flag or config key.
- **Every Discord answer carries a footer with the model and the time, tokens and cost only on the owner's own runs, and long answers are split at 2000 characters without breaking code fences or sent as one embed** — [#297](https://github.com/CorvidLabs/Corvidinho/pull/297) (DISCORD-15, DISCORD-15.a, DISCORD-16; REQ-discord-075, REQ-discord-457, REQ-agent-073): the answer footer showed only the model and the run plumbing, the live status showed `~tok` to everyone, chat and button-pick answers were cut at 1800 characters by the Discord spawn client and `/work` and `/session start` answers at 1500 by #282's `clipPostSummary`, and the gateway and slash replies sliced at 1900. The footer-only embed of a chat answer, button-pick resume, `/work` or `/session start` answer (edited in place or a reply fallback, on its last part) now reads `model | [tokens | cost |] time | state=… verified=… attempts=…`. The time is the run's elapsed time (`42s`, `3m 05s`, `1h 02m`), frozen when the answer first goes out, so an edit that adds the SAFE-8 notice keeps it. Tokens and cost show only when the acting user is the configured owner (`isOwnerDiscord`; with no owner configured, nobody sees them): tokens are the run's provider-reported usage from its last `usage` frame in compact form (`2k tokens`), cost is that usage at the SAFE-8 list price for the exact model id with no cache discount, and missing usage or a 0-token report prints `tokens unknown | cost unknown`, and a model with no known price prints the tokens with `cost unknown`, never `$0`; `delegate` and `council` workers are not counted. The live thinking status's `~tok` now shows on the owner's runs only. The bridge takes the whole answer from the result frame (still capped at 4000 characters) and cuts it at 6000 at most (`DISCORD_ANSWER_MAX`, ending in `…`, a closing role note kept) after the SAFE-6 scrub. Over 2000 characters it is split along line breaks into parts of at most 2000: a part that ends inside a code block closes it and the next reopens it with the same language, a block that opened partway through a part moves whole to the next, an over-long line is cut at its last space in the back half of the part, else hard (never inside a surrogate pair), and the ROLES-CHAT-3 note stays whole in the last part. Plain prose over 2000 and up to 4096 characters with no code fence, no user or role mention, no button and nobody to ping goes out as one embed holding the text and the footer instead. The first part is the thinking message edited in place (or the fallback or deferred slash reply); later parts are fresh posts that reply to nothing and never ping from model text; on a reply fallback a user the answer must ping (the SAFE-8 owner line, a clarify requester) is pinged once, by the part that holds the mention; the footer and any Choose or Answer button ride the last part (a Choose stub still has no footer); a reply to any part continues the session. The SAFE-8 80% warning and the SAFE-13 notice (#295) are appended within the 6000 cap instead of cutting the answer to 1900, and the gateway's channel-reply and message-edit caps and the slash reply cap go from 1900 to 2000 (DMs and button and form replies keep 1900). When a later part of an answer edited in place cannot be posted, the answer is delivered in part and the bridge logs `[discord] answer part <n>/<m> for <session> not sent`. WATCH (the WATCH spawn client still reads the answer at 1800, and its summary comment clips it at 1200) and schedule posts (1500, `clipPostSummary`, no footer) are unchanged. No env var, config key, slash command, schema or protocol change.

### Discord

- **An ask button press passes the actor gate and the mute / rate limit, like chat and slash** — [#232](https://github.com/CorvidLabs/Corvidinho/pull/232) (DISCORD-6, ALLOW-5, DISCORD-DENY-3, ROLES-CHAT-1, IDENTITY-1; REQ-discord-010, REQ-discord-201): a press on a Choose button checked only the channel gate before running the agent, so a muted or deny-listed user, or an unlisted one, could keep a session going through buttons, and the open button showed them the choices. After the channel gate, every press (open and pick) now runs `gateActor` (a refusal gets the ephemeral zero-width ack) and then the shared mute / rate check with the presser's permission level (`You do not have permission to interact with this bot.` / `Slow down! Please wait before sending more messages.`, ephemeral); both refusals return before the ask is touched, so it stays pending and nothing runs or posts. Presses now carry the member's role ids, so a member allowed only by role is let in and a deny-listed role is refused. Every press uses one slot of the shared per-user budget (`DISCORD_RATE_LIMIT_MAX`, default 10 per `DISCORD_RATE_LIMIT_WINDOW_MS` 60 s, unless `DISCORD_RATE_LIMIT_BY_LEVEL` sets a cap for the presser's level), including a press on someone else's or an expired ask; a full @mention → open → pick round uses 3, and a press the channel, actor or mute / rate gate refuses uses none.
- **Outbound posts parse no mentions from model text** — [#269](https://github.com/CorvidLabs/Corvidinho/pull/269) (DISCORD-8, ROLES-CHAT-3, ROLES-CHAT-8; REQ-discord-205): only ask pings set allowed mentions, so chat answers, collapsed answers, `/session start` and `/work` answers, ask-button replies, schedule posts and the `discord-post-message` REST post went out with Discord's default parsing, and text steered by a non-owner or public GitHub content could make the bot ping `@everyone`, `@here`, a role or a user. The live client now defaults to `{ parse: [], repliedUser: true }` and every payload sets it explicitly (reply, edit, embeds, slash reply / editReply, component reply / update); `@everyone` / `@here` are defanged with a zero-width space before the gateway's length cut; `discord-post-message` sends `allowed_mentions: { parse: [] }` and defanged text. An ask still pings its requester or the owner by id, and a reply still pings the author it answers. Role and user syntax stays visible as text.
- **A deny-listed thread under an allowlisted parent is refused, silently, on every path** — [#276](https://github.com/CorvidLabs/Corvidinho/pull/276) (DISCORD-5, ALLOW-3, ADMIN-3.c, DISCORD-DENY-1, DISCORD-DENY-2, DISCORD-DENY-3; REQ-plugins-005, REQ-discord-212, REQ-discord-311, REQ-discord-476): with `channels = [parent]` and `deny_channels = [thread]`, the router, the ask-button gate, restart recovery and `discord-send-file` let the allowlisted parent outvote the deny, so an @mention in the denied thread started a session and posted there. New `isMonitoredConversation` returns false when either the thread or its parent is deny-listed, else true when either is allowlisted; the MessageCreate gate, the press gate and restart recovery use it. A message in the denied thread gets no reply and no session, a talk there is not continued, a press gets only the zero-width ack (the allowlist tip for an admin) with the ask left pending, and a recovered row there is deleted with no edit or reply. A deny on the parent also wins over an explicitly allowlisted thread. `discord-send-file` refuses a deny-listed thread (`is denied`). Slash, `/schedule create`, the scheduler tick and `discord-post-message` already gated the thread id itself and are now locked in by tests; slash still checks only the thread id, not its parent. A deny added mid-run does not stop that run's final answer.
- **Ask option ids are unique, so Choose buttons open and a pick resumes with the pressed label; a reply after a button ask timed out clears it** — [#284](https://github.com/CorvidLabs/Corvidinho/pull/284) (DISCORD-ASK-1, DISCORD-ASK-3, DISCORD-ASK-5; REQ-agent-045, REQ-discord-044): `normalizeAskOptions` never de-duplicated ids, so a repeated model id, two ids equal once cut to 32 characters, or a position fallback matching an earlier id gave two buttons one `custom_id`; Discord rejected the Choose press, and a press on the second option resumed with the first label. A repeated id now takes the first unused position number, claimed only once its label survives; asks whose ids are already unique come out byte-identical, so stored asks and open buttons keep working. And a timed-out button ask stayed pending, so every thin reply restated it with a requester ping and a dead Choose button: any continue except a cancel now clears a pending button ask past `expiresAt` first, then a thin reply restates the newest open ask that has not timed out, or runs the agent when none is left; a cancel keeps its short ack. Free-text asks are unchanged.
- **A press on an ask that is no longer open answers `that choice expired`** — [#283](https://github.com/CorvidLabs/Corvidinho/pull/283) (DISCORD-ASK-5, DISCORD-ASK-8; REQ-discord-045): a press on an earlier ask dropped past its timeout when a newer one was picked, or on an ask whose session was TTL-purged (at runtime or while loading after a restart), got `This choice isn’t for you (or it was already answered).` The session store now remembers closed asks in memory only (ask id, requester, expiry, channel and thread ids, never the question or option text; the newest 1000), and the channel and thread stand in for the missing session at the channel gate, so a late press in the talk's thread is judged as a live one there. After the actor and mute / rate gates, the requester's press on a closed ask gets the ephemeral `that choice expired`, with no run and nothing posted; another user's press still gets not-for-you, as do a re-press after a pick, a press after `cancel` and a press on a talk ended by `/session end`. A restart forgets closed asks except those of talks it purges while loading.
- **Every cap on the way to a post keeps the closing `(not allowed for your role)` note** — [#282](https://github.com/CorvidLabs/Corvidinho/pull/282) (ROLES-CHAT-3, ROLES-CHAT-2, SAFE-8; REQ-discord-734, REQ-watch-734): a non-ADMIN run whose tool call was refused ends its summary with `(not allowed for your role)`, and later head cuts dropped it. The WATCH summary comment's 1200-character clip (after the secret scrub, which still runs first), the schedule run row and its `✅` / `❌` post, the `/work` and `/session start` answers (a 1500 cut that #297 above then lifts), and the SAFE-8 80% warning append now cut the body before the note; new `clipPostSummary` caps a post summary at `POST_SUMMARY_MAX` 1500 and at what fits after the post's head within the 1900-character reply cap, so the gateway's cut never reaches the note. A summary without the note is cut exactly where it was before. The 400-character stuck-ask context clip is unchanged.

### WATCH

- **Assignment and review-request events are gated on the user who assigned or requested** — [#288](https://github.com/CorvidLabs/Corvidinho/pull/288) (ALLOW-1, ALLOW-2, ALLOW-5; REQ-watch-302): WATCH checked only the thread author, so a collaborator not on the GitHub user allowlist could assign the watch user to an allowlisted author's issue or PR, or request its review there, and start a session and agent run. WATCH now reads the actor from the thread's issue events (`issues.listEvents`, page 1 plus the newest pages, the same 10-page cap as comments): the newest `assigned` / `review_requested` event naming the watch user, taking `assigner` / `review_requester`, else the event `actor`. The router checks the repo, then the author, then that actor with the same user allowlist (deny wins); a missing or non-allowlisted actor is refused (`actor_not_allowlisted`), filtered before the per-issue dedupe, counted in `[watch] poll cycle … refused=N`, with no ack and no run, and cannot shadow a trusted comment on the same issue. A non-rate-limit lookup error leaves no actor (refused); a rate limit is rethrown so the poll backs off as before. The author stays the session user. A bot or GitHub Action that assigns or requests review needs its own login on the user allowlist. Every poll reads the issue's events once for each searched item assigned to the watch user and once more for each PR awaiting its review. Each read is page 1 plus up to 9 of the newest pages, so up to 10 requests, and it happens even when that event id was already handled. A refused `assign-…` / `reviewreq-…` id is kept in memory and stays refused until the watcher restarts, so an allowlisted user who later re-assigns the watch user, or requests its review again, on the same issue or PR is not picked up until then.

### Scheduler and audit

- **A zero cron step (`*/0`) is refused as a `CadenceError` instead of hanging the bridge** — [#278](https://github.com/CorvidLabs/Corvidinho/pull/278) (DISCORD-SCHEDULE-4; REQ-discord-020): `/schedule create cadence:"*/0 * * * *"` looped forever in the cron parser, froze the bridge with no reply and stopped its HEAR ingress and scheduler ticks (WATCH runs in its own `corvidinho github watch` process and was not affected). A step below 1 in any field (including inside a comma list, and `n/0`) now throws `Invalid cron step in "PART": the step must be 1 or more.` before any loop runs; `/schedule create` answers it ephemerally and stores nothing, and store create, resume and claim throw the same error. Range loops now stop at the field's maximum, so a huge end (`0-99999999999`, or one past 2^53) resolves at once and the existing rules decide (the 5-minute minimum, or `No matching cron date found within 366 days`). Every cadence with a step of 1 or more resolves to the same times as before. A single value with a zero step (for example `5/0`), which was accepted before as plain `5`, is now refused. A schedule already stored with one throws that `CadenceError` each time a tick tries to claim it: the bridge logs `[scheduler] tick failed: Invalid cron step in "5/0": …` and the daemon logs a `tick.failed` event. The throw ends that tick early, so due schedules created after it do not start, and neither do the pending-ask delivery and backup steps that come after the claims. Pause or delete the schedule and create it again without the `/0`.
- **Schedule posts name the project, not its absolute host path; a tampered unkeyed audit chain reads `chain BROKEN at #N` without a key** — [#287](https://github.com/CorvidLabs/Corvidinho/pull/287) (SAFE-5; REQ-discord-353, REQ-plugins-095, REQ-discord-418): the title that starts every schedule `✅` / `❌` post and schedule ask post (including the pre-run stuck ask and asks the delivery pass posts for daemon runs) printed the stored `project` as is, so a schedule on an absolute path showed the host path in the channel. It now shows `projectLabel(project)`, the last segment of an absolute path or a relative name as given, as `/schedule list` already did for anyone but ADMIN (the owner still sees the full path in `/schedule list`, but in these channel posts everyone, the owner included, sees only the name); the run row keeps the full error and the model's prompt still gets the stored project. Without `CORVIDINHO_AUDIT_HMAC_KEY`, the audit line said `cannot verify keyed rows` for every failure, including a tampered chain with no keyed rows; it now reads `Audit: <n> entries · chain BROKEN at #<seq>` when the break comes before any keyed row, and `cannot verify keyed rows (CORVIDINHO_AUDIT_HMAC_KEY not set)` only when verify reached a keyed row. This line shows in the bridge start log, `/status` and `/admin config show`; `verifyAudit` is unchanged and still fails closed.

### Allowlists

- **A leading `~` in `CORVIDINHO_ALLOWLIST_FILE` is the bot's HOME, so the documented example no longer silently drops the file's deny lists** — [#280](https://github.com/CorvidLabs/Corvidinho/pull/280) (ALLOW-4, GITHUB-6; REQ-plugins-006): `.env.example` documents `CORVIDINHO_ALLOWLIST_FILE=~/.config/corvidinho/allowlist.toml`, Bun's `.env` loader keeps the `~`, and the resolver used the value as written, so it looked for a cwd-relative `~/…` path, never found it and used env overlays only with no error: the file's `deny_repos`, `deny_users` and `[owner]` were dropped while an env allow list still admitted targets, `/admin` would have written to a literal `./~/.config/…`, and doctor said only `[info] … not found — env overlays only`. A trimmed value of `~` now resolves to HOME and one starting `~/` to a path under HOME (the same HOME the default path uses; `env.HOME || homedir()` for `/admin`); `~user`, absolute, relative and inner-`~` values are used as written. Every reader goes through that one resolver: the bridge, WATCH, the GitHub repo gate, the Discord plugins, `git-push`, the owner loader, the daemon, `/admin` and doctor. A missing explicit file still means env overlays only. `.env.example` now says a leading `~` or `~/` is the bot's HOME (not `~user`) and any other path is used as written. Upgrade note: a bot whose `.env` already sets `CORVIDINHO_ALLOWLIST_FILE=~/…` and has a file at that HOME path starts applying that file's allow and deny lists and `[owner]` after this release. If that file cannot be read or parsed, the bridge, `github watch` and `corvidinho daemon` refuse to start (fail closed). Run `corvidinho doctor` after updating: the `allowlist-file` line should read `<HOME>/… loads (values not shown)`, not `not found — env overlays only`.
- **`discord-post-message` and `discord-send-file` accept channels allowlisted only through `DISCORD_CHANNEL_IDS`, like the bridge** — [#272](https://github.com/CorvidLabs/Corvidinho/pull/272) (ALLOW-3, DISCORD-5, DISCORD-17; REQ-plugins-009, REQ-discord-004): both tools checked only the allowlist file and `CORVIDINHO_DISCORD_ALLOW_CHANNELS`, so in a channel listed only in `DISCORD_CHANNEL_IDS` the bridge listened but the tools refused (exit 3: `Discord channel allowlist empty` when the file and `CORVIDINHO_DISCORD_ALLOW_CHANNELS` listed no channel, else `is not allowlisted`), bridge-started runs included. Both now check `mergeChannelIds` (file + `CORVIDINHO_DISCORD_ALLOW_CHANNELS` + `DISCORD_CHANNEL_IDS`), the list bridge ingress, slash, `/schedule`, scheduler ticks and doctor already used; `denyChannels` is still checked first (`is denied`, exit 3), and a malformed or unreadable allowlist file still refuses. No channel beyond the bridge's own is reachable.

### CLI

- **`plugins run` passes every argument after `--` to the plugin verbatim** — [#268](https://github.com/CorvidLabs/Corvidinho/pull/268) (REQ-cli-186, REQ-cli-143): the global parser consumed `--non-interactive`, `--json`, `--no-verify`, `--task`, `--tier` and `--max-retries` anywhere in argv, `--json` was stripped even after `--`, and `--help` / `-h` anywhere printed help, so `plugins run search-grep --json -- --no-verify src`, `plugins run shell-exec -- gh pr list --json number` and `plugins run shell-exec -- ls -h` misbehaved. Parsing now stops at the first `--` after `plugins run <name>` and passes the rest as-is; a `--` elsewhere keeps its meaning, `--task` still takes the next token verbatim, and `--project` is read only before the `--`. Help is read from the parsed argv, so `task run --task -h` runs the task instead of printing help. Flags before the `--` work as before.
- **Operator docs match the code: empty Discord user and role allowlists admit anyone in an allowlisted channel, and DAEMON.md lists every log event** — [#273](https://github.com/CorvidLabs/Corvidinho/pull/273) (ALLOW-3, DISCORD-5, DISCORD-SCHEDULE-3, CLI-8; REQ-cli-005, REQ-cli-108, REQ-discord-005): `corvidinho --help` and the go-live checklist (printed by `doctor` and `discord bridge`) said empty user/role lists were deny-all. `--help` now has a channel row (`HEAR channel allowlist; empty = refuse start`) and a users/roles row (both empty = anyone in an allowlisted channel; once either is set, only those users, role holders and the owner), and its header reads `default-deny`. `docs/DAEMON.md` gains `daemon.start_failed` (start refused, exit 1, lock released) and `spend.warning` log rows, and its allowlist row now describes the schedule creator gate. `tests/docs.operator-facts.test.ts` fails when a daemon log event has no DAEMON.md row. No behaviour change.

### Plugins and agent

- **The SAFE-3 `cd` clamp reads the scripts a command runs** — [#233](https://github.com/CorvidLabs/Corvidinho/pull/233) (SAFE-3; REQ-plugins-087): `firstDisallowedCd` checked only the command text, so `./bad.sh`, `sh bad.sh`, `. ./bad.sh`, `sh < bad.sh`, a here-doc fed to `sh`, `cat bad.sh | sh` and `bash < <(cat bad.sh)` could `cd /etc` unchecked. The clamp now reads each script a command sources (`.` / `source`, `BASH_ENV=`, `--rcfile` / `--init-file`), feeds to a shell (script operand, `<` / `<>` input, here-doc, here-string) or runs by path (a `#!` naming a shell, or text with no `#!`, also behind `env`, `exec`, `timeout`, `xargs` and `find -exec`), from the root and every in-root `cd` target before it; a refusal names the script (`/etc (in ./bad.sh)`), exit 2. It refuses what it cannot check: a script path that would expand, a missing sourced or shell-run file, more than 1 MiB of script text, 32 scripts or 32 directories to look in, a script the same command writes, shell input that would expand, and a shell reading a pipe, inherited stdin or a process substitution. `trap 'cd /etc' EXIT`, `sh -c - '…'` and `bash -co pipefail '…'` are closed too, and every alias definition now refuses, not only `alias c=cd`: `alias ll='ls -l'` is refused as `ll=ls -l (alias)`. Fail-closed means scripts that `cd` through a variable or source another script through one now refuse, like the same command typed directly: `./gradlew`, `./mvnw`, many `./configure`s, `cd "$(dirname "$0")"` and `scripts/corvidinho-update.sh`; `for f in *.sh; do bash "$f"; done` and `curl … | sh` refuse too. The shell is still never offered to the model from the allowlist. The busy-lock child-process tests get a 30 s timeout.
- **Verify retry feedback never ends on half a surrogate pair, a non-git lead verifies after a delegate worker that left no result, PR attribution matches the real footer, and a blank GitHub token counts as missing** — [#290](https://github.com/CorvidLabs/Corvidinho/pull/290) (AGENT-4, FLEDGE-2, CLI-4; REQ-agent-002, REQ-agent-502, REQ-plugins-003, REQ-plugins-051, REQ-cli-003): `verifyFeedbackExcerpt` could keep an emoji `error:` line ending on a lone high surrogate when its scan end fell inside a pair; the scan end now steps back one character. A `delegate` worker killed at its timeout, aborted, crashed or never started leaves no result frame, and a non-git lead then saw no files and ended `done` with verify skipped; a delegate result without a boolean `verified` now counts as unreported edits, so the lead fails closed and runs verify (a non-git project with no verify lane now ends `failed` there instead of `done`). `github-pr-create` treated any body containing "Made with" and "Corvidinho" as attributed; it now looks for the exact markdown or plain attribution. Doctor's `github` line and `getGithubToken` trim `GITHUB_TOKEN` / `GH_TOKEN` as WATCH does: a whitespace-only token prints `[missing] github`, no longer shadows a real `GH_TOKEN`, and is never sent to Octokit; a token with a trailing newline is sent without it. Because that `github` line is not ok, `corvidinho doctor` now exits 1 on a box whose only GitHub token is whitespace, and the updater's doctor step rolls that update back. Such a box never had a usable token: WATCH already treated it as missing.

### Tests

- **Security gates get tests that fail when the gate is removed** — [#277](https://github.com/CorvidLabs/Corvidinho/pull/277) (SAFE-2, ALLOW-2, ALLOW-5, GITHUB-6, ROLES-CHAT-8, DISCORD-8; REQ-plugins-493, REQ-plugins-004, REQ-plugins-083, REQ-plugins-182, REQ-watch-003, REQ-discord-012): SAFE-2 refuses `files-write` / `files-edit` / `files-delete` and a `git-commit` deletion for every `specs/` path, not only `*.spec.md`; `deny_users` and `deny_orgs` win over allow lists in the WATCH router, and `deny_orgs` wins in `git-push` (exit 3, no remote ref created); a community session's real Octokit visibility lookup refuses a private repo (no pulls call) and an unconfirmed one (404, no token); and `verifyRequesterCanSend` runs discord.js's own `permissionsFor` with no injected checker (View Channel + Send Messages, not in guild, missing channel, Attach Files). Each test fails when its gate is disabled. REQ-plugins-493 records the existing fail-closed private-repo refusal. No product code change.
- **SAFE-5 / SAFE-6 tests catch audit chain tamper on any column, dangerous-run error rows, every re-scrub target and the `/status` audit line** — [#279](https://github.com/CorvidLabs/Corvidinho/pull/279) (SAFE-5, SAFE-6; REQ-plugins-095, REQ-discord-066, REQ-discord-095): an edit to any one of the ten stored `audit_log` columns (`ts` through `hash`, every column except `seq`) breaks the chain at that row; a dangerous plugin that fails records `started` then `error` with its exit code (1 for a throw, which still reaches the caller); each text column REQ-discord-066 names is in `SCRUB_TARGETS` and reads `[redacted:<kind>]` after a re-scrubbing open; and the bridge start log and `/status` show the line `verifyAudit` computes from the bridge's own DB and key, including tampering after start. No product code change.

### Ops

- Package version **0.0.34** — restart the Discord bridge, `corvidinho daemon` and watch after update. **Schema v11 → v13**: the first open of the shared DB after update, by any Corvidinho process, runs two forward-only migrations in order, each recording its version in `schema_meta` (`version`) once it has run: v11 → v12 (#291) creates the `forget_requests` table (ids, status and timestamps only) with `idx_forget_requests_status` and the unique partial index `idx_forget_requests_one_pending` (one pending ask per person); v12 → v13 (#296) creates the `conversation_threads` table (`id`, `surface`, `thread_key`, `user_id`, `session_id`, `project`, `summary`, `turns`, `participants`, `bot_message_ids`, `updated_at`) with `idx_conversation_threads_thread` (surface, thread key, user), `idx_conversation_threads_session` and `idx_conversation_threads_updated`. Later opens read version 13 and run neither. Both only create new tables and indexes (`IF NOT EXISTS`) and alter no existing table; `SCRUB_RULES_VERSION` stays 3, so there is no re-scrub pass (`conversation_threads.summary` and `turns` are scrubbed on write and are SAFE-6 re-scrub targets from now on). Backup state lives in `schema_meta` `ops_*` keys, not a table. A build with an older schema opens a v13 DB without refusing it: its `version < N` steps all skip, it leaves the stored version at 13 and ignores the tables it does not know, so the bridge, daemon and watch need not restart together. v0.0.34 gets its tag and Release from CI when this bump lands on main.
- New operator surface: env vars `CORVIDINHO_BACKUP_DIR` (optional; absolute, outside any git repo; restart the bridge and daemon after changing it) and `CORVIDINHO_LLM_CONTEXT_TOKENS` (optional; the model's context window in tokens, default 8192 when unset or not a positive integer, raised to 1024 when lower; no upper bound, but the conversation part of a prompt never passes 32,000 characters, which a window of 10,000 tokens already reaches; read by the bridge and `github watch` at start, so restart them after changing it; see `.env.example`); `[people.<id>]` sections with `display`, `nicknames`, `discord_ids`, `github_logins`, `github_ids` and `role = "team" | "community"` in the allowlist file (read live, no restart; see `allowlist.example.toml`; declare yourself by putting the owner's Discord id in your entry, or the owner keeps Discord-id memory); slash subcommands `/admin people list|add|link|unlink|remove|role` (owner-only, audited; still nine top-level commands, and the bridge re-registers the slash set on ready); CLI `corvidinho backup list` and `corvidinho backup restore <snapshot> <target> [--force]`; plugins `discord-send-file` (dangerous: only once named in `CORVIDINHO_ALLOWLIST`), `github-docs-read`, `github-milestone-list`, `memory-profile` and `memory-forget-me` (read or non-dangerous, no allowlist entry); a `backup` line in `corvidinho doctor`; `hi/ops.md` (OPS-1/2). Spawn env keys Corvidinho sets on each spawned run, not operator config: the bridge and `corvidinho daemon` (schedule runs) always overwrite all seven — `CORVIDINHO_DISCORD_REPLY_CHANNEL_ID`, `CORVIDINHO_DISCORD_REPLY_PARENT_CHANNEL_ID`, `CORVIDINHO_ACTING_ROLE`, `CORVIDINHO_ACTING_WORK_TASK`, `CORVIDINHO_ACTING_GITHUB_LOGIN`, `CORVIDINHO_ACTING_GITHUB_ID`, `CORVIDINHO_ACTING_GITHUB_REPO` (the three GitHub keys blank); WATCH always overwrites the two reply-channel keys (blank) and the three `CORVIDINHO_ACTING_GITHUB_*` keys (the commenter and the thread's repo) but does not set `CORVIDINHO_ACTING_ROLE` / `_WORK_TASK`, and its runs stay community because it sets no acting Discord user.
- Before naming `discord-send-file` in `CORVIDINHO_ALLOWLIST`, give the bot **Attach Files** in the target channels (without it Discord refuses the upload) and turn on **Server Members Intent** in the Developer Portal: every attach runs the DISCORD-8 check (View Channel, Send Messages and Attach Files for the acting user) through a short-lived Guild Members login and fails closed when that login is refused. For forget-me cards the owner must share a server with the bot and accept DMs from its members; otherwise each ask lapses as a no after 24 h and the bridge logs `[discord] direct message failed: …`. No new gateway intent is requested.
- If `CORVIDINHO_ALLOWLIST_FILE` uses the `~/…` form, 0.0.34 starts applying that file (its allow and deny lists, `[owner]` and `[people]`); a malformed file then makes the bridge, watch and daemon refuse to start and doctor print `[fail] allowlist-file`, which the updater's doctor step turns into a rollback. Check it first on 0.0.33 by running `corvidinho doctor` with the absolute path in `CORVIDINHO_ALLOWLIST_FILE`. On WATCH, a bot or GitHub Action that assigns the watch user or requests its review needs its own login on the GitHub user allowlist, or those events are refused (`refused=` in the poll log, no ack, no run).
- To turn on backups, set `CORVIDINHO_BACKUP_DIR` and an `/announce` channel (failure notices go only there; without one they wait and doctor warns). `corvidinho doctor` prints `[warn] backup: off …` while `CORVIDINHO_BACKUP_DIR` is unset, then a `[warn]` backup line naming the missing `/announce` channel until one is set, and never fails on the backup line. Restore with `corvidinho backup list`, stop the bridge, the daemon and `github watch` (each holds the shared DB open, and a held target is refused even with `--force`), then `corvidinho backup restore <snapshot> <target> --force`.
- Behaviour operators will see: a team or community message, topic or `/work` description that trips the SAFE-13 detector gets a public `🛡️ I won't act on that …` reply pinging the owner and runs nothing (on WATCH, a tripped title or body gets one comment @mentioning the `[owner]` GitHub login, a `[watch] SAFE-13 refused …` log line and a `refused=` count, and no run); when a scanned tool result trips it (`web-fetch`, the GitHub readers, `discord-user-lookup`, or a `delegate` / `council` worker's own hit), that run, including the owner's runs and a CLI `task run`, loses every mutating tool and `memory-store` for the rest of the run; each hit writes an `injection-suspected` audit row; model text no longer pings `@everyone`, `@here`, roles or users (`@everyone` / `@here` are shown defanged; role and user mentions still render but notify nobody); WATCH and schedule runs, `council` voices and the `delegate` workers of a bridge, WATCH or schedule run stay non-owner (community) whoever triggered them (a `delegate` worker of an operator CLI `task run` still has no role session, as before); operator `plugins run shell-exec` refuses scripts that `cd` through a variable or source a script through one, such as `./gradlew` and `scripts/corvidinho-update.sh` (refused as `${SCRIPT_DIR}/lib/update-helpers.sh (script path would expand) (in scripts/corvidinho-update.sh)`; run those outside `shell-exec`).
- Conversations and answers (#296, #297): a continued Discord chat now replays up to about 26,000 characters of conversation at the default window (at most 32,000 with a larger `CORVIDINHO_LLM_CONTEXT_TOKENS`), where it replayed at most 6000, and a WATCH follow-up on an issue or PR now carries that thread's kept conversation, so continued and follow-up runs send more prompt tokens and cost more per run; the SAFE-8 cap still applies. The 80% is measured at 4 characters a token on the conversation part only; the system prompt, tool schemas, project instructions and the identity and memory blocks are added on top of it. A talk's own user replying to one of its answers after it ended or idled out, or posting a plain message in its thread, within 30 days of its last activity, now starts a new session and a run where the message was ignored before. `conversation_threads` keeps each conversation's scrubbed summary and last 20 turns for 30 days after its last activity (purged on every read and write of the table, when the bridge loads its sessions, hourly in a running bridge and on each watch poll cycle; `corvidinho daemon` alone never purges it). Answer footers now read `model | <time> | state=… verified=… attempts=…` for everyone and add `<n> tokens | $<cost>` after the model (`<n> tokens | cost unknown` for a model with no known price, `tokens unknown | cost unknown` when no usage came back) only on the owner's own runs, and a non-owner's thinking status no longer shows `~tok`. Chat, button-pick, `/work` and `/session start` answers are no longer cut at 1800 or 1500 characters: up to the result frame's 4000 arrive whole, over 2000 as several messages (fences intact, the footer on the last) or, for plain prose up to 4096 characters, one embed. WATCH comments and schedule posts are unchanged. New log lines: `[discord] conversation <step> failed: …` (steps `summary read`, `read`, `write`, `retain`, `resume`, `purge`, `forget`), `[discord] session thread rewrite for <session> failed: …`, `[watch] conversation purge failed: …` / `[watch] conversation read failed: …` / `[watch] conversation write failed: …`, and `[discord] answer part <n>/<m> for <session> not sent`.
- Rollback to 0.0.33: it opens the v13 DB without complaint (its migrations stop at v11, it has no check for a newer version and leaves the stored 13 alone) and ignores `forget_requests`, `conversation_threads` and the `ops_*` keys, so pending forget asks are neither delivered nor decided until 0.0.34 is back; kept conversations are neither resumed nor purged (a reply after the TTL is ignored again, WATCH follow-ups carry only the newest event, and 0.0.34 purges the stale ones once it runs again), a live session that was condensed replays only the turns 0.0.34 kept (its summary lives in `conversation_threads`), `CORVIDINHO_LLM_CONTEXT_TOKENS` is ignored, and answers go back to one message cut at 1800 characters (1500 for `/work` and `/session start`) with the model-and-plumbing footer; the 0.0.33 allowlist loader reads `[people.*]` sections as unrelated sections, so declared people and roles have no effect: team members lose their review tools and `/work` edits and are plain non-ADMIN users, like everyone but the owner. Memory 0.0.34 stored under `person:<id>` or `project:<key>` scopes stays in the DB but 0.0.33 reads only Discord-id scopes, so it does not show it; and 0.0.33's Discord auto-inject (the newest 20 rows) and `memory-recall` read a Discord-id scope whatever the category, so a `private` note stored there (an undeclared user's, or the owner's while not declared under `[people]`) would be injected into that person's own runs again. Snapshots written by 0.0.34 are ordinary v13 SQLite files; 0.0.33 has no `backup` command. A `~/…` `CORVIDINHO_ALLOWLIST_FILE` goes back to being read as a cwd-relative path that is never found, so 0.0.33 silently drops the file's allow and deny lists, `[owner]` and `[people]` and uses env overlays only (the owner only from `CORVIDINHO_OWNER_*` env); put the absolute path there before rolling back.
- Lifecycle only: [#293](https://github.com/CorvidLabs/Corvidinho/pull/293) re-checked, reviewed and archived the six SpecSync changes that #273, #282, #283, #287, #288 and #289 left in `verifying` after their squash-merges; no product code, docs, schema or version change.
- `persona.md` (PERSONA-2, #274) is new at the repo root, with `hi/persona.md`. Only the copy committed at `HEAD` loads, so change the voice by commit or PR: a hand edit on the VM is not loaded, and the updater's `git checkout --force` discards it. A run whose persona is missing, empty, refused, truncated or edited but not committed still runs and emits one `Persona: persona.md …` note: a CLI `task run` prints it on stderr (a `Text` frame with `--output ndjson`), while the bridge, `corvidinho daemon` and WATCH drop that frame, so it is neither posted nor logged there. The file is capped at 8 KiB; a longer one is cut and the note says `truncated`.
- `GITHUB_TOKEN` / `GH_TOKEN` are now trimmed (#290): when neither holds a non-blank value (one set to whitespace and the other unset or blank), `corvidinho doctor` now also prints `[missing] github` (0.0.33 printed `[ok] github` there). Its `github-watch` line already trimmed and already failed on such a box (`WATCH needs GITHUB_TOKEN/GH_TOKEN`), so doctor already exited 1 and the updater's doctor step already rolled back; this update changes no doctor exit code. What does change: a whitespace-only `GITHUB_TOKEN` no longer shadows a real `GH_TOKEN` for the Octokit plugins, and a token with a trailing newline is sent without it. A non-git project with autonomous `delegate` runs and no verify lane now ends `failed` after a worker that timed out, was aborted, crashed or never started.

## 0.0.33

### Discord posts and open asks

- **`discord-post-message` checks the Discord user the run acts for, not an id the model passes** — [#264](https://github.com/CorvidLabs/Corvidinho/pull/264) (DISCORD-8): the tool read the requester only from its own `--requesting-user-id` / `--requester` flag, which the model fills in, and ran the requester check only when one was passed. Since 0.0.32 an allowlisted `discord-post-message` is offered to the model in the owner's runs, and the bridge already sets each run's acting user in `CORVIDINHO_ACTING_DISCORD_USER_ID`, but the tool never read it: a post without the flag checked only that the bot could post, a flag naming someone else ran the check for that user instead, and strict mode (`CORVIDINHO_DISCORD_REQUIRE_REQUESTER_CHECK=1`) accepted any id the model supplied. In a run the bridge started (chat, `/session start`, `/work`), after the channel allowlist (still checked first), the requester check (View Channel and Send Messages on the target channel) now always runs for the acting user, with or without the flag, and that check also satisfies strict mode. Any `--requesting-user-id` or `--requester` value naming a different user, including a second value beside the acting user's (the other alias or a repeated flag), is refused (exit 3, `refused: --requesting-user-id / --requester names a different Discord user than the one this run acts for. … leave the flag out. Nothing was posted.`) and no check runs for it; the acting user's own id is accepted. A denial refuses with the existing reason and fix hint, now naming the acting user, and a user the member lookup cannot find is refused as not in the guild. When the check cannot run (its short-lived login with the Guild Members intent is refused because Server Members Intent is off, times out or errors), the post fails closed: refused (exit 3) with one scrubbed reason line (SAFE-6) that names Server Members Intent and never the token, and nothing is posted. A dry run (`CORVIDINHO_DISCORD_DRY_RUN=1`) reports the acting user as the requester. Outside the bridge (operator `corvidinho plugins run`, a local `task run`, where no acting user is set) nothing changes: the check runs only for a passed flag, strict mode refuses a post without one, and a check that throws still throws. WATCH runs have no acting user and are not ADMIN, so ROLES-CHAT-3 refuses the tool there before it runs.
- **Open asks are secret-scrubbed when a session is saved, and the SAFE-6 re-scrub now reaches them** — [#265](https://github.com/CorvidLabs/Corvidinho/pull/265) (SAFE-6): every other stored session text was scrubbed of vendor-key-looking secrets, but a session's open asks were not. `discord_sessions.pending_ask` (the model-written question, option labels and option ids, as one JSON object, or an array when several asks are open) was saved raw, and the re-scrub did not include the column, so a token in a question or a choice stayed in the DB. Saving a session now scrubs each open ask's question and option labels, and its option ids as a backstop, to `[redacted:<kind>]`, in both the one-object and the array row; askId, expiresAt, option ids and stubMessageId are stored byte-identical, so open buttons keep working. When an ask is made, an option id the model chose that looks like a secret (an id keeps up to 32 letters, digits, `_` and `-`, enough for a whole AWS key id) is replaced by the option's position (`1`, `2`, …), the fallback an id with no usable characters already got, so neither the button nor the stored row carries it. Only the saved copy is scrubbed: the live session keeps the ask as the model wrote it until the session is reloaded from the DB (for example after a bridge restart), the same as the session topic. `SCRUB_RULES_VERSION` goes from 2 to 3, so the next open of the shared DB re-scrubs stored rows once, now including `pending_ask`, which it reads as JSON: every string value is scrubbed, key names are kept, and the row is rewritten (still valid JSON) only when a value changed, so a question holding a private-key block with no END line can no longer cut the JSON; unchanged rows stay byte-identical, the rewritten rows still load as the session's open asks, and a second open changes nothing. An ask stored by an older build with a secret-looking option id has that id redacted like any other text. A stored value that is not valid JSON is scrubbed as text, kept and counted, with one warning per column, `[scrub] discord_sessions.pending_ask: N stored value(s) were not valid JSON; scrubbed as text`, that never shows the stored text. Posted questions and button labels are not scrubbed by this change.

### WATCH

- **A rate limit on WATCH's own comments now holds the next poll** — [#266](https://github.com/CorvidLabs/Corvidinho/pull/266) (WATCH-RELIABILITY-3): the GitHub rate-limit backoff covered only the poll fetch. When the auto-ack or run-summary comment failed, the post kept only the error message, and neither comment helper throws, so a 403 with `retry-after`, `x-ratelimit-remaining: 0` or a rate-limit message, or a 429, on either comment set no backoff: the log showed only `[watch] ack failed …` / `[watch] summary failed …` and the next poll ran on the normal interval. A failed comment post now keeps its HTTP status and only the three rate-limit headers (`retry-after`, `x-ratelimit-remaining`, `x-ratelimit-reset`), and the poller sends it through the same backoff as a rate-limited fetch: `Retry-After`, else `x-ratelimit-reset`, else the documented 60 s default, with the existing `[watch] github rate-limit backoff ms=… until=… reason=…` line right after the failure line. The backoff only ever moves later. A poll inside it is skipped (`[watch] poll skip rate-limit backoff remaining_ms=…`, no fetch) and the poll loop waits it out. The current cycle is not cut short: the agent still runs once for the event whose ack was rate-limited, and later events in that cycle (at most `maxTriggersPerCycle`) still try their own comments. A plain 403 on a comment (no rate-limit signal) still only logs the failure. WATCH-RELIABILITY-1 is unchanged: no summary after a failed ack, and a failed ack or summary is not retried after the backoff.

### Ops

- Package version **0.0.33** — restart the Discord bridge, `corvidinho daemon` and watch after update. **No schema change** (still v11), and no new env var, config key, slash command, CLI flag, command or plugin. The first open of the shared DB after update, by any Corvidinho process, runs one SAFE-6 re-scrub pass (`SCRUB_RULES_VERSION` 3) that rewrites the stored open asks in `discord_sessions.pending_ask` whose question, labels or ids hold a secret; the pass takes the DB write lock as earlier re-scrubs did, and later opens skip it. Nothing here needs the bridge and daemon restarted together. v0.0.33 gets its tag and Release from CI when this bump lands on main.
- If `CORVIDINHO_ALLOWLIST` names `discord-post-message`, turn on **Server Members Intent** for the bot in the Discord Developer Portal before updating. Every post the owner's runs (chat, `/session start`, `/work`) make with it now logs in a short-lived client with the Guild Members intent to check that the owner can view and send in the target channel; when that login is refused, times out or errors, the post fails closed (refused, exit 3, one scrubbed line naming the intent) and nothing is posted. The owner's runs can post only where the owner could post themselves. `docs/DISCORD-GO-LIVE.md` (step 3 and the E.3 table) and `docs/discord.md` say the same.
- In a bridge run, a `discord-post-message` call whose `--requesting-user-id` or `--requester` names anyone but the acting user is refused (exit 3) and nothing is posted; the refusal tells the model to leave the flag out. Operator `corvidinho plugins run discord-post-message` and a local `task run` behave as before, unless `CORVIDINHO_ACTING_DISCORD_USER_ID` is set in their env, in which case the bridge-run rules apply to that id.
- WATCH now holds its next poll when the auto-ack or run-summary comment hits a GitHub 403/429 rate limit (`Retry-After`, else `x-ratelimit-reset`, else 60 s), logging `[watch] github rate-limit backoff …` right after `[watch] ack failed …` / `[watch] summary failed …`. The comment that failed is not posted again after the backoff, and a plain 403 on a comment still only logs the failure.
- Open asks saved from now on hold `[redacted:<kind>]` where the question or a choice label had a secret, and an ask restated after a bridge restart shows that label. An open ask stored by 0.0.32 with a secret-looking option id has that id redacted by the pass, so that one choice's button stops matching. A `pending_ask` value that is not JSON gets one `[scrub] discord_sessions.pending_ask: N stored value(s) were not valid JSON; scrubbed as text` warning at that open. After a rollback to 0.0.32 the rewritten rows keep the same JSON shape and load as before, 0.0.32 sees scrub rules version 3 recorded and does not re-scrub, and asks it saves are stored raw again.

## 0.0.32

### Discord asks and sessions

- **A button-pick resume tells the model who pressed the button** — [#253](https://github.com/CorvidLabs/Corvidinho/pull/253) (IDENTITY-4): a chat message, `/session start` and `/work` pass the acting user's Discord display name and username into the prompt's identity block, but the run resumed by picking an option on a Choose ask passed only the Discord id and the owner, and the button interaction carried no names. A non-owner's resumed prompt therefore had the snowflake and no `display_name` line, although the turn before it had one. The live gateway now reads the presser's names from the button press itself (display name from the guild member's display name, then nickname, then global name, then user display name; the username; both trimmed, and a blank one left out), and the pick resume passes them to the identity inject the way the chat path does. A non-owner's resumed prompt gets their display name, or their username when there is none; the owner still gets the owner map display; a press with no names known injects the id only, never an invented name. The names come from the press, so a renamed user shows under their current name, and nothing new is stored. Scheduled runs still inject no identity.
- **Earlier Choose buttons keep working when a later message asks again** — [#254](https://github.com/CorvidLabs/Corvidinho/pull/254) (SESSION-MULTI-3): a session held a single pending ask. When the same user kept chatting while a Choose ask was open and that run asked a new question, the new ask replaced the open one, and the earlier Choose and option buttons answered `This choice isn’t for you (or it was already answered).` before any press or timeout. Asks are now kept by their ask id. A new ask becomes the session's current one (the one a thin reply such as `ok` restates and a free-text reply answers), and a button ask it supersedes stays open beside it, so its buttons still open the choices and resume the session with that ask's question until pressed or expired (about 30 minutes). A press is matched to its own ask among all of the session's open asks. A pick, a late press (`that choice expired`) or a free-text answer clears only that ask; when the current ask is cleared, the newest remaining ask that has not timed out takes its place, and earlier ones already past their timeout are dropped instead of restated. A superseded free-text ask is still replaced, and `cancel` / `nevermind` / `forget it` still clears every open ask of the session. The open asks persist in `discord_sessions.pending_ask`: one JSON object when one ask is open (as before), a JSON array, oldest first, when several are. They survive a bridge restart, and the schema version is unchanged.
- **`/work` and `/session start` ask with the Choose button when the choices fit a short list** — [#255](https://github.com/CorvidLabs/Corvidinho/pull/255) (DISCORD-ASK-1, DISCORD-ASK-4): only @mention / reply chat used the Choose button. A `/work` or `/session start` run that stopped with a clarify or stuck question stored it without its options and posted the question, with any numbered choices in it, as public text to be answered by reply, even when the `ask-human` tool had listed options. Now, when the choices can be listed (two or more `ask-human` options, else two or more numbered, lettered or bulleted lines in the question; at most the first five become buttons), the one answer message is the chat's public Choose stub (`❓ I need your input before I can continue.` mentioning the requester, or `⚠️ I'm stuck and need a human.`, then `Press **Choose** to answer privately.`) with a single Choose button. The session's pending ask keeps the options and the answer's message id, so the requester's Choose press opens the options ephemerally and the pick resumes that same session in the stub, through the path chat already uses. The button stays on the fallback reply and when the owner notice is appended by re-editing the answer. The stub's message id is recorded only while that ask is still pending on a live session, so a session that ended in between is not written back. A question whose options cannot be listed stays a free-text ask, a SAFE-8 spend-cap stop is never a button ask or pending, a stuck ask still pings the owner in a separate post (the stub itself pings nobody then), and schedules still post text.
- **The final answer keeps the model and run state in a small footer** — [#260](https://github.com/CorvidLabs/Corvidinho/pull/260) (DISCORD-3.a): when a run finished, the bridge normally edits the thinking message into the answer (DISCORD-ASK-7), and that edit cleared the embed, so the model and the run's plumbing line (`state=… verified=… [verifySkipped] [cancelled] attempts=…`) were shown nowhere. They appeared only on the fallback path, when the edit was unavailable or failed and the bridge set the Done / error status and posted a separate reply. The collapsed answer (an @mention reply, the answer to a button-pick resume, `/session start` and `/work`) now keeps one footer-only embed with no description, `model | state=… verified=… [verifySkipped] [cancelled] attempts=…`, green or red exactly as the fallback status would be (red for a failed run that asks no question, or a stuck ask). The answer text stays plain human text with no plumbing. A Choose stub (the message with buttons) still carries no embed (DISCORD-ASK-6), a later re-edit that appends the SAFE-8 owner notice keeps the same footer and colour, and when neither the model nor the plumbing is known no embed is sent. Nothing beyond the model and the plumbing line is shown.

### CLI

- **`--project <path>` runs Corvidinho in another project without `cd`** — [#257](https://github.com/CorvidLabs/Corvidinho/pull/257) (CLI-5): every command read the current directory and there was no way to point it elsewhere. `corvidinho --project P doctor` exited 1 with `Unknown command: --project`, and `task run … --project=P` silently dropped the flag and ran in the start directory, with its `fledge.toml`, its specs and the `.env` Bun had loaded from there. The new global flag `--project <path>` (or `--project=<path>`, before or after the command, anywhere before a `--`; the last one wins) makes the process run as if it had been started in `<path>`. Before any command runs, it rebuilds the environment the way Bun would for a process started there: a one-off Bun probe in `<path>` (15-second cap, Bun config pinned to `/dev/null`, so the project's `bunfig.toml` is never read) loads `<path>`'s `.env`, `.env.<NODE_ENV>` and `.env.local` with `$VAR` expansion, starting from the environment the process was launched with (`/proc/self/environ`). Variables really set in the environment win, and the start directory's `.env` values do not carry over. The CLI's own Bun `--no-env-file` / `--env-file` flags are passed on, so `bun --no-env-file src/cli.ts --project P` loads no `.env` from P. The process then changes into `<path>`, so `task run` reads that project's `fledge.toml` and specs and every other command uses it as the project root, and tools the CLI starts without an explicit env (`specsync`, `fledge run spec-check`, git) get the project's env, not the start directory's. `discord bridge`, `github watch` and `daemon` take `<path>` as their project root, so their agent binary defaults to `<path>/src/cli.ts` (set `CORVIDINHO_BIN` when `<path>` is not a Corvidinho checkout). A missing, non-directory, unreadable or empty `--project`, or one whose `.env` files cannot be loaded, stops with one `corvidinho: --project …` error line and a hint (exit 1; `{ ok: false, error }` with `--json`) and changes nothing. Spawned agents never get `--project` and keep `--no-env-file`, and env values are never printed.
- **`doctor` and a new report-only `init` name missing project files** — [#258](https://github.com/CorvidLabs/Corvidinho/pull/258) (CLI-4): `doctor` checked keys, `fledge` / `specsync` on PATH, plugins, the allowlist file, the owner, the data dir and spend, but nothing about the project, so `task run` in a directory without `fledge.toml`, a verify lane, `.specsync/` or `specs/` failed at the verify gate after the model had done the work. `corvidinho init` was `Unknown command: init` (exit 1). `doctor` now prints four more lines after `fledge` / `specsync`, read from the current directory: `fledge.toml` (present and valid TOML), `verify-lane` (`[lanes.verify]` in `fledge.toml` or a `.fledge/lanes/*.toml` import reaches the `spec-check` task, or a step or task dependency that runs `specsync check`), `.specsync` and `specs`. A missing item is a `[missing]` line in plain language that says what fails without it and, where Fledge or SpecSync has one, the command that creates it (`fledge run --init`, `specsync init`, `specsync generate`; no tool creates a verify lane with spec-check, so that line says what to add). A verify lane that needs a task no `[tasks]` table defines is `[missing]` and names the task (up to three), since fledge refuses to run that lane. Run from a subdirectory of a git project whose root has the item, the line points to that root instead of asking you to create a new one. Any `[missing]` line fails doctor (exit 1). The new `corvidinho init` is report only: it prints the `llm` line (a `[warn]` without a key, never a failure), `fledge`, `specsync` and the same four project lines, creates and changes nothing, exits 1 only when an item is missing, and points to `corvidinho doctor` for Discord / GitHub keys and allowlists. File contents and TOML parser messages are never printed (SAFE-6), and a `fledge.toml` or lane import that is a FIFO or device is never opened.

### Agent tool catalog and roles

- **A non-owner's invented write call gets the role refusal, and the reply says so** — [#256](https://github.com/CorvidLabs/Corvidinho/pull/256) (ROLES-CHAT-3): a non-ADMIN session (a non-owner in Discord, WATCH, schedules) is offered no mutating tool, so any mutating call the model makes there is one it invented. The tool loop answered those with the generic `refused: tool "<name>" is not offered in this run's catalog (SAFE-1 / capability tier)`, which never reached the role gate, and nothing in the run's summary said a call had been refused for the caller's role. Now a call to a registered mutating or dangerous plugin that was not offered, from a caller who is not ADMIN at that call (re-checked against the live owner config, ROLES-CHAT-6), gets the same refusal `runPlugin` gives, `Denied: plugin "<name>" is not allowed for your role (ROLES-CHAT-3).` (exit 2), and still never runs. Once any call in a task run is refused for the caller's role (an invented call, or an offered call refused because ADMIN was lost mid-run), every summary of that run ends with the line `(not allowed for your role)`, added once and not when the summary already says it. The 4000-character NDJSON result cap and the 1800-character Discord chat body cap now cut the body before that closing line and keep the line. Only the exact role refusal for the called name counts, so a failed `delegate` worker whose summary quotes the phrase does not add it to an ADMIN lead's summary. A name that is not a registered plugin, ADMIN runs and local CLI runs keep the catalog refusal and get no line, and the live progress still shows an invented name as `(unknown tool)` and never shows the refusal. The WATCH summary comment preview (1200 characters) and the scheduled-run post (1500) clip the body again and can still drop the line, and a run that ends in a question posts the question without it.
- **`task run` offers allowlisted dangerous tools to the model; the shell and the runners still wait on SAFE-3** — [#261](https://github.com/CorvidLabs/Corvidinho/pull/261) (CLI-3, GITHUB-1, GITHUB-3, ROLES-CHAT-4, PLUGIN-3): the model's tool catalog left out every dangerous plugin and no product caller turned that off, so a `CORVIDINHO_ALLOWLIST` entry never reached the model. The GitHub writes, `memory-forget` / `memory-override`, `files-delete`, the git writes and every `fledge-*` command could only be run through `corvidinho plugins run` (and the `/work` draft-PR step). A dangerous plugin is now offered to the model when the run's allowlist (`CORVIDINHO_ALLOWLIST`, which spawned runs inherit from the bridge) names it exactly and its `minTier` fits the run's tier; an unlisted one stays out, and a call to it is still refused as not offered. `shell-exec`, `node-exec`, `python-exec` and `cargo-exec` stay out even when listed, until the SAFE-3 decision, and still run through `corvidinho plugins run`. The role, SAFE-9 and tier filters and every runtime gate are unchanged (SAFE-1, the GITHUB-6 repo allowlist, the SAFE-4 two-phase confirm, the ADMIN re-checks), so only ADMIN runs (the owner's Discord chat, `/session start` and `/work`) and a local `corvidinho task run` get these tools; non-owner chats, WATCH, schedules and council voices never do. An empty allowlist gives the old catalog. Fledge commands are discovered (a `fledge` subprocess) only when the allowlist names a `fledge-*` command and the session is not a non-ADMIN one. A `delegate` worker gets the lead's allowlist, so a local run's worker is offered the same tools and a role session's worker none. A Fledge command can change files without reporting them, so in a project that is not a git work tree, a run that called one (or a local run's `delegate` worker, which could have) now runs the verify lane anyway, with the note `Verify gate: no git working tree to diff, and <names> may have changed files no tool reported, so verifying anyway.`, and is never done on a failed lane (AGENT-4). In a git work tree the real diff already covers them.
- **Fledge itself is a set of typed plugin commands** — [#262](https://github.com/CorvidLabs/Corvidinho/pull/262) (PLUGIN-1): Corvidinho bridged the project's Fledge *plugins* (`fledge-<command>`) but none of Fledge's own commands, so `lanes list`, `lanes validate`, `lanes run` and `run <task>` were reachable only through `shell-exec` or inside the verify gate. Four builtins now run the local fledge CLI with an argv array (no shell) in the project root or task worktree: `fledge-lanes-list` (`lanes list --json`, a typed `{count, lanes}` result) and `fledge-lanes-validate` (`lanes validate --json [--strict]`, a typed `{valid, strict, laneCount, errors, warnings}` result) are read-only (not dangerous, any tier, also offered to non-ADMIN sessions); `fledge-lanes-run <lane>` and `fledge-run <task> [args…]` are dangerous at code tier. A lane or task name must match `^[A-Za-z0-9][A-Za-z0-9_.:-]{0,63}$`, so model argv never becomes a fledge option (`--init` would write `fledge.toml`); validate takes only `--strict`, and `fledge-run` passes the task's args after `--` verbatim. fledge is looked up on absolute PATH entries only when a command runs (exit 127 when missing) and gets the verify lane's scrubbed env without `CDPATH` / `OLDPWD`, stdin closed, a 30 s (reads) or 10 min (runs) timeout (exit 124), 64 KiB output caps per stream, and its process tree killed when the calling run is aborted (exit 130); output is secret-scrubbed. Before a read starts fledge, each lane source (`fledge.toml`, `.fledge/lanes/*.toml`) must resolve inside the real project root and not to a secret path, else the call is refused (exit 2). A project Fledge plugin command named `run`, `lanes-list`, `lanes-validate` or `lanes-run` is skipped (`plugins list` shows `skipped fledge-run: name already registered by builtin`). The two runs join `shell-exec` and the runners in the SAFE-3 hold-out: a task run never offers them to the model, even when allowlisted, until the SAFE-3 decision; they run through `corvidinho plugins run` with an allowlist entry. Allowlisting a core builtin starts no Fledge plugin discovery.

### Ops

- Package version **0.0.32** — restart the Discord bridge, `corvidinho daemon` and watch after update. **No schema change** (still v11), and no new env var, config key or slash command. The CLI gains one global flag, `--project <path>` (#257), and one command, the report-only `corvidinho init` (#258). Four plugin commands are new: `fledge-lanes-list`, `fledge-lanes-validate`, `fledge-lanes-run` and `fledge-run` (#262); a project Fledge plugin command with one of those names is now skipped. Nothing here needs the bridge and daemon restarted together. v0.0.32 gets its tag and Release from CI when this bump lands on main.
- Review `CORVIDINHO_ALLOWLIST` before updating: every dangerous tool it names, except `shell-exec`, `node-exec`, `python-exec`, `cargo-exec`, `fledge-lanes-run` and `fledge-run`, is now offered to the model in the owner's Discord runs (chat, `/session start`, `/work`) and in a local `corvidinho task run`; non-owner chats, WATCH and schedules still get none. There is no separate switch, so the usual `/work` entries `git-commit,git-push,github-pr-create` also let the owner's runs commit, push and open a PR themselves, before the run's verify (the `/work` PR step itself still waits for verify). A `fledge-*` entry makes those runs discover the project's Fledge commands (they start `fledge`), and in a project that is not a git work tree such a run verifies even when no tool reported a changed file.
- In non-owner chats, WATCH and scheduled runs, a reply now ends with `(not allowed for your role)` when the model tried a write tool it was not offered; nothing runs. The line is kept when a long Discord reply is cut, but the WATCH summary preview (1200 characters) and the scheduled-run post (1500) can still cut it off, and a run that ends in a question posts the question without it.
- `corvidinho doctor` now fails (exit 1) in a directory that lacks `fledge.toml`, a verify lane that runs spec-check, `.specsync/` or `specs/`. The box updater's doctor gate includes these lines (`docs/BOX-UPDATE.md`), so an update rolls back if the checkout lacks one; Corvidinho's own checkout has all four. Run doctor from the project directory or with `--project <path>`. `corvidinho init` reports the same project lines plus the LLM key, Fledge and SpecSync, and creates nothing.
- With `--project <path>`, the start directory's `.env` values are dropped and `<path>`'s `.env` files are loaded instead; variables set in the real environment (shell, systemd `EnvironmentFile`) still win. `discord bridge`, `github watch` and `daemon` started with `--project` use `<path>` as their project root and spawn `<path>/src/cli.ts` unless `CORVIDINHO_BIN` is set, so set it when `<path>` is not a Corvidinho checkout.
- `/work` and `/session start` now answer a question whose choices fit a short list with the Choose button: the requester presses **Choose**, picks privately, and the pick resumes the session in that message. A stuck question still pings the owner in a separate post, and schedules still post text. Collapsed answers (chat, a button-pick resume, `/work`, `/session start`) now carry a small footer-only embed with the model and `state=… verified=… attempts=…`.
- A session with several open Choose asks now stores them as a JSON array in `discord_sessions.pending_ask`. A 0.0.31 bridge reads such a row as no pending ask, so after a rollback those buttons answer `This choice isn’t for you (or it was already answered).`; a row with one open ask reads as before.

## 0.0.31

### Discord sessions, autocomplete and presence

- **A second user in a thread no longer takes over the first user's session** — [#244](https://github.com/CorvidLabs/Corvidinho/pull/244) (SESSION-MULTI-1, SESSION-MULTI-2, DISCORD-2.a): the bridge's thread index held one session per thread, so when user B @mentioned the bot in a thread where user A already had a session, B's new session replaced A's entry and A's next plain message there was dropped without notice (`ignore`, `no_mention`). After a bridge restart, whichever session loaded last held the thread, and when B's session ended the thread entry went with it, so A's plain messages were dropped from then on too. The in-memory thread index is now keyed by thread and Discord user, and a plain message in a thread continues only its author's own session there: A and B each continue their own, and an open button ask stays with its session (same ask id and expiry) while another user talks. This holds after the other user's session ends and after a restart. A plain message from someone with no session in the thread still starts nothing (an @mention starts theirs), and a deny-listed or unlisted actor is still refused silently whenever any session exists in the thread. The `discord_sessions` rows already stored the thread and the user, so there is no schema change.
- **Channel autocomplete lists channels only for the owner in an allowlisted channel** — [#245](https://github.com/CorvidLabs/Corvidinho/pull/245) (DISCORD-DENY-3, ADMIN-4): Discord shows `/admin` and `/announce` to every guild member, and the gateway answered their channel autocomplete with no check. Any member typing into `/admin channels remove` got the allowlisted channel names and ids, and `/admin channels add` or `/announce channel` listed every guild text channel the bot can see, from any channel. A muted owner and an owner with a deny-listed role got the same lists, and so did everyone when no owner was configured. Every autocomplete request is now re-checked against the live config in the slash gate order (channel allowlist, then the actor gate, then ADMIN with the live mute set; ADMIN is the configured owner), and anyone who fails gets an empty choice list: no channel names, ids or allowlist entries, and no allowlist tip. If the gate is not wired, says no or throws (logged as `[discord] autocomplete gate failed: …`), the answer is also empty. The owner in an allowlisted channel keeps the same choices as before (guild text channels for `add` and `/announce`, the live allowlist for `remove`). Autocomplete does not use a rate-limit slot. Command registration is unchanged, so members still see the commands, and running one still gets the reply it got before.
- **The version status survives a gateway re-identify** — [#252](https://github.com/CorvidLabs/Corvidinho/pull/252) (DISCORD-12): the bridge built its discord.js client with intents only and set the `vX.Y.Z` Custom Status in the ready handler. discord.js sends the client's `presence` option (empty by default) on every gateway IDENTIFY, and ready does not fire again after a non-resumable re-identify (invalid or expired session), so after one the bot showed no version under its name until the bridge restarted. The client is now built with the version presence (online, Custom Status `vX.Y.Z`), so the first IDENTIFY and every re-identify carry it; ready still sets the same presence, and a failed set is still only logged. The DISCORD-8 requester check that `discord-post-message` runs for a requesting user id logs in with the same bot token on its own short-lived gateway session, and its IDENTIFY sent an empty activity list under the bot name; it now sends the version presence too. The IDENTIFY path is tested against discord.js with the socket stubbed, not against live Discord.

### Scheduler asks and audit

- **A schedule that pauses itself, or cannot start a run, pings the owner** — [#249](https://github.com/CorvidLabs/Corvidinho/pull/249) (AUTONOMY-2, AUTONOMY-4, DISCORD-SCHEDULE-3): a schedule paused after 5 failed runs in a row with no word about it. The bridge posted the pausing run's usual `❌ … failed (exit N)` line with no ping, and a run claimed by `corvidinho daemon` posted nothing. A run whose project could not be resolved or whose worktree could not be created was recorded failed and posted nothing, so such a schedule failed silently on every tick and then paused silently. Both cases now record a stuck ask on the run row and go out through the 0.0.30 ask path (the bridge posts its own run's ask at once; a daemon run's ask is posted by the bridge's next tick), so the owner is pinged, once per question. The run that causes the pause records `Paused after 5 failed runs in a row. Fix the cause, then resume it with /schedule resume.` in place of its own ask, plus `Last failure: …` when that run stopped with a question of its own. In the bridge this post replaces the `❌` line, and for a run without its own question it shows only the `failed (exit N)` line as context. The ask is chosen from the same SQL failure count, in the same transaction that records the run, so a stale cached count (a bridge and a daemon on one data dir) cannot pause the schedule without recording it. A run that cannot start posts a fixed question with no host path (`Could not start this run: the schedule's project could not be resolved.` or `Could not start this run: its worktree could not be created.`), and the full error stays in the run row's `error` and the daemon's `run.finished` log; a resolve or worktree step that throws (for example EACCES or ENOSPC) takes the same path. While the same failure repeats, the question is posted again without a new ping. If the bridge's post of a pause ask resolves false or throws, the ask is handed back for the next delivery pass, because a paused schedule has no next run. A run that throws and makes the 5th failure posts the pause ask at once, with no context. Runs refused by the DISCORD-SCHEDULE-3 creator or channel gate still record no ask of their own; when they cause the pause, its ask stays pending until the gate lets it through. The daemon logs `run.needs_human` (`reason` `stuck`) for all of these runs.
- **`/schedule delete` is on the audit trail and fails closed** — [#250](https://github.com/CorvidLabs/Corvidinho/pull/250) (SAFE-5): `/schedule delete` removes a schedule and its whole run history, but it wrote nothing to the SAFE-5 audit chain and went through even when no trail was available. It now follows `/admin`: before deleting it appends a `started` row (action `schedule-delete`, surface `discord:schedule`, actor the invoker's id, an args digest of the resolved schedule id, never the raw id). If that row cannot be written (the trail throws, including a keyed chain on a process without `CORVIDINHO_AUDIT_HMAC_KEY`, or no trail is wired), it replies `Refused: audit log unavailable (SAFE-5): <reason>. Nothing changed.` and deletes nothing.
